Preface

“Vinayo nāma sāsanassa āyu, vinaye ṭhite sāsanaṃ ṭhitaṃ hoti” — In accordance with these words of the Great Arahants who conducted the Recitations, the Vinaya Pitaka, being the teachings of the Tathagata’s authority, is the very life of the Dispensation. Since the Dispensation of the Buddha endures only as long as the Discipline endures, all bhikkhus devoted to the Dispensation must study the Vinaya Pitaka with the utmost reverence. The formal acts found in the Vinaya Pitaka, such as the establishment of boundaries and Higher Ordination, are called Ecclesiastical Acts (Vinaya Kamma). Since the entry of clansmen into the Buddha’s Dispensation through Higher Ordination, and the repeated purification of the virtue of those who have entered, must be accomplished through the path of Ecclesiastical Acts, these acts hold a supreme position in the Dispensation.

When performing Ecclesiastical Acts, they must be done with extreme purity, without deviating from the methods prescribed in the Vinaya. An Ecclesiastical Act performed incorrectly is as if it were not performed at all. Even if done through ignorance, performing an Ecclesiastical Act incorrectly is a grave evil; it leads to the destruction of the Buddha’s Dispensation. If a boundary is established incorrectly, even if stone pillars are erected, it is not a valid boundary. Not a single Ecclesiastical Act performed therein will be valid. Even if a Higher Ordination is conducted there, those being ordained do not become fully ordained (Upasampanna). In the future, any ordinations or Higher Ordinations conducted by those who were ordained in such an invalid boundary will also be invalid. Even if the boundary is valid, if the act of Higher Ordination itself is performed incorrectly, they remain unordained. The pupils ordained by them in the future will likewise remain unordained. The gravity of the harm caused by performing Ecclesiastical Acts incorrectly must be understood from these facts.

To gain thorough knowledge of Ecclesiastical Acts, one must master the entire Vinaya Pitaka. Otherwise, the Ecclesiastical Acts must be studied separately. Merely studying books is not sufficient to perform Ecclesiastical Acts correctly; one must also have practical experience in performing them. In the past, many bhikkhus possessed both knowledge and practical experience in Ecclesiastical Acts. It must be said that today, bhikkhus with thorough knowledge and practical experience are not common. There are several reasons for this.

The bhikkhu of the past could devote his entire time to the study of the Dhamma and Vinaya. The modern bhikkhu, in order to live with a certain status in the world, is compelled to learn many things besides the Dhamma and Vinaya. Therefore, the modern bhikkhu must spend the greater part of his period of study on subjects outside the Dhamma and Vinaya. This is one reason for the deficiency in knowledge of Dhamma and Vinaya among contemporary bhikkhus. Furthermore, a bhikkhu who returns to his monastery after completing his studies at a Pirivena must devote his entire time to temple affairs, the needs of the lay supporters, the business of the Community, and the work of providing for his own requirements. This too is a reason for the deficiency in knowledge of Dhamma and Vinaya.

Another reason why Sri Lankan bhikkhus are unpracticed in Ecclesiastical Acts is that the act of Higher Ordination in various Nikāyas has been monopolized by specific groups. The act of Higher Ordination was not intended by the Tathagata to be the exclusive right of only one group. It belongs to all bhikkhus who possess Higher Ordination. In any region outside the Middle Country (Majjhima-desa), five bhikkhus who possess Higher Ordination may assemble within a boundary and perform the act of Higher Ordination.

During the time of the Buddha, candidates for Higher Ordination in various regions were ordained by the bhikkhus residing in those very regions. Even now in Burma, the act of Higher Ordination in various regions is performed by the local bhikkhus. There is no specific group or specific place designated solely for the act of Higher Ordination. Higher Ordinations performed everywhere are accepted without distinction. Since the act of Higher Ordination is performed in all parts of the country, all bhikkhus gain the practice of performing Ecclesiastical Acts by having the opportunity to participate in them. If that custom were to prevail in Sri Lanka as well, many bhikkhus would naturally receive training in Ecclesiastical Acts.

One can speak of both the merits and demerits of the act of Higher Ordination being restricted to one group. Whatever merits it may have, there is also a most dangerous disadvantage. That is: if the group holding the right to ordain should somehow become an impure group, the Higher Ordination would be lost to the entire Nikāya—to the whole lineage of the Community in that Nikāya. The best way for a candidate to obtain Higher Ordination is to receive it from a group whom he knows well and can accept without doubt as possessing pure Higher Ordination. A bhikkhu who has obtained Higher Ordination in this way will have no doubt regarding his own status. That lack of doubt becomes a reason for him to guard his virtue with devotion. One who receives Higher Ordination from an unknown group whose details are not known will not have firm confidence in his own Higher Ordination. Often, doubts (kukkucca) arise regarding the ordination. This is a great obstacle and a cause for discouragement for a clansman who desires to fulfill the monastic life well.

Another disadvantage of the act of Higher Ordination being restricted to one group is that candidates and their teachers are forced to provide a large amount of money. As things stand today, Higher Ordination cannot be obtained without spending money. The Tathagata, who laid down training rules prohibiting the use of money by monastics, would not have prescribed a Higher Ordination that cannot be obtained without spending money. He prescribed Higher Ordination such that any poor candidate possessing only a bowl and robes could obtain it without spending a single cent.

Today, the practice regarding Higher Ordination is the exact opposite. Since Higher Ordination has become something to be obtained by spending money, it has become a custom in the country to send letters to relatives and lay supporters to obtain funds for the act of Higher Ordination, just as laypeople send wedding invitations to relatives and friends. During the seasons of Higher Ordination, some people receive not just one or two, but many such letters. This is a burden to the country and a cause for dissatisfaction among many. Furthermore, when the act of Higher Ordination is performed only by a specific group within each Nikāya, it allows others to charge that the entire Nikāya lacks valid Higher Ordination based on some error, thus intensifying sectarian divisions. Therefore, may the Great Community of Bhikkhus consider the suitability or unsuitability of the act of Higher Ordination being restricted to specific groups!

Since the facts to be known regarding Ecclesiastical Acts are not mentioned in a single chapter of the Vinaya Pitaka, it is not easy to learn everything necessary by reading the Vinaya texts. For bhikkhus who do not frequently use the Vinaya texts, it is difficult even to find the places where those Ecclesiastical Acts are mentioned. Therefore, for the benefit of bhikkhus who have little experience in Ecclesiastical Acts and little familiarity with the Vinaya texts, so that they may easily know how to perform those acts and perform them correctly, this volume titled "Ecclesiastical Acts" was compiled on the occasion of the Sambuddha Jayanti.

In this work, the procedure for Going Forth and the act of Higher Ordination, numerous Ecclesiastical Acts performed for the purification of the virtue of ordained bhikkhus, many acts such as the Act of Banishment performed as punishment for erring bhikkhus, the Act of Turning Over the Bowl performed as punishment for laypeople, many Ecclesiastical Acts performed for the convenience of the Community and individuals, and the difficult act of establishing a boundary have been presented in an easily understandable manner. With the help of this book, even a group of bhikkhus unskilled in the Vinaya will be able to perform an act of Higher Ordination correctly. All Ecclesiastical Acts currently performed among bhikkhus and those that bhikkhus may have to perform on various occasions are included here without omission.

The recitation of the formal act (kammavākya) must be done with great care. If an error occurs during the recitation, the act is not valid. Therefore, those who respect the Vinaya recite the formal act while looking at the text. To enable even elderly Maha Theras to read them easily, the formal acts have been printed in large type.

Printing a book of this nature, which uses several types of fonts, is a time-consuming and difficult task. However, we mention with joy that through the efficiency of Mr. W.D.J. Ratnayake Appuhamy, the manager of the Anula Press in Maradana, the work on this book was completed in a very short time.

May merit accrue to our pupil, Hengoda Kalyanadhamma, who performed with great care all the tasks such as proofreading that an author must do during the printing of a book.

May the Buddha's Dispensation shine forth!

Yours, desiring the stability of the Dispensation,

Venerable Rerukane Chandavimala Thera.

October 10, 2499/1955

At Sri Vinayalankararama, Pokunuwita.

Going Forth

Shaving (Bhandukamma)

The first Vinaya procedure that bhikkhus must perform when admitting a faithful clansman—who desires to realize Nibbana—into this noble, emancipating Dispensation established by our Blessed, Worthy, and Rightly Self-Awakened One to release helpless beings wandering in Samsara and burning with the fires of suffering, is called ‘Shaving’ (Bhaṇḍukamma). It is a Vinaya procedure concerning the shaving of the hair of candidates for the Going Forth (pabbajjā).

During the time the Buddha was dwelling [in the world], a certain son of a family quarreled with his parents, fled from home, and went to a monastery. There, he received the Going Forth from a certain Elder. This was done privately, and many bhikkhus were unaware of it. The parents of the child, searching for him, arrived at that monastery and asked the bhikkhus they encountered, “Venerable Sirs, have you seen such a child? Do you know where such a child has gone?” Because those bhikkhus had not seen the child, they said, “We have not seen him,” and because they did not know, they said, “We do not know about that child.”

The parents, not being satisfied with this, searched here and there and eventually saw the child who had gone forth. Becoming angry with the bhikkhus, they reviled them, saying, “These bhikkhus say they have not seen what they have seen, and they say they do not know what they know; they are liars. These lying bhikkhus are shameless (alajjī) and immoral,” and so forth. The bhikkhus reported this matter to the Tathagata. Thereupon, the Blessed One, so that such a thing might not happen in the future, prescribed the Shaving procedure, saying:

“Anujānāmi bhikkhave! saṅghaṃ apaloketuṃ bhaṇḍukammāya” (I allow you, monks, to ask the Community for the shaving.)

This procedure should be performed when the hair of one who has hair longer than two finger-breadths is being cut by a bhikkhu or by the order of a bhikkhu. Perform it in this manner: Assemble all the bhikkhus residing within the monastery and bring the candidate for the Going Forth before the Community. The teacher or another bhikkhu shall then announce before the Community:

“Saṅghaṃ bhante! imassa dārakassa bhaṇḍukammaṃ āpucchāmi” (Venerable Sirs, I ask the Community for the shaving of this boy.) Recite this sentence once, twice, or thrice. For the Shaving, it is also proper to say “Imassa dārakassa bhaṇḍukammaṃ āpucchāmi” or “Imassa samaṇa karaṇaṃ āpucchāmi” or “Ayaṃ samaṇo hotu kāmo”.

Without assembling the bhikkhus, it is also proper to go to the places where the individual bhikkhus reside and inquire regarding the Shaving. The preceptor himself need not go to the bhikkhus; it is also proper to send another to inquire from them. If the procedure must be performed at a time when bhikkhus are so numerous that it is difficult to assemble them all or to inform them individually, go to a formal boundary (baddha-sīmā) or a water-boundary (udakukkhepa-sīmā) and perform the procedure in the presence of the bhikkhus assembled there.

The shaving of the head of one who is to go forth is a significant matter. If the person going forth is one of merit, virtue, and wisdom, who can be of special benefit to the Sasana in the future, the teachers should take special care regarding such individuals. When ordaining such a person, the best method is for the teacher himself to take the candidate to the place of water and shave the head, rather than sending the candidate away to shave and bathe alone.

The moment of going forth is a time when a clansman's mind is very clear and confident. Therefore, what is taught at that time is deeply impressed upon his mind. The occasion of shaving the head is the most opportune time to instruct the candidate in the ‘Meditation on the Fivefold Skin-parts’ (taca-pañcaka-kammaṭṭhāna). Certain clansmen with the requisite supporting conditions (upanissaya-sampatti), by reflecting on the taca-pañcaka-kammaṭṭhāna taught at this moment, directing the mind accordingly, defining mind and matter (nāma-rūpa), and seeing the three characteristics of impermanence etc. in formations, realize the Four Noble Truths and attain Arahantship through the stages of the Path and Fruit even while the hair is being shaved, destroying all defilements and realizing Nibbana. There are many mentions in our texts of individuals who became Arahants while their hair was being shaved.

Even for clansmen who do not have the supporting conditions to become Arahants at that very moment, what is taught then is of great benefit as it is deeply impressed upon the mind. To ensure the candidate benefits from this occasion, the teacher should shave the head while instructing him in the taca-pañcaka-kammaṭṭhāna. If the teacher is unable to shave the head and has another do it, the teacher should stand nearby and instruct the candidate in the taca-pañcaka-kammaṭṭhāna. Alternatively, the teacher should appoint another bhikkhu to do so. In doing this, first place the lock of hair that is cut into the hand of the candidate, tell him to look at it well and take its nature to heart, and then instruct him in the meditation. Instruct him as follows:

The Meditation on the Fivefold Skin-Constituents

  1. These hairs of the head, being a part of the body: are loathsome because of their foul smell, because they originate from impurities such as bile, phlegm, blood, pus, feces, and urine, and because they arise within the heap of thirty-one impurities.
  2. These hairs of the body, being a part of the body: are loathsome because of their foul smell, because they originate from impurities such as bile, phlegm, blood, pus, feces, and urine, and because they arise within the heap of thirty-one impurities.
  3. These nails, being a part of the body: are loathsome because of their foul smell, because they originate from impurities such as bile, phlegm, blood, pus, feces, and urine, and because they arise within the heap of thirty-one impurities.
  4. These teeth, being a part of the body: are loathsome because of their foul smell, because they originate from impurities such as bile, phlegm, blood, pus, feces, and urine, and because they arise within the heap of thirty-one impurities.
  5. This skin, being a part of the body: is loathsome because of its foul smell, because it originates from impurities such as bile, phlegm, blood, pus, feces, and urine, and because it arises within the heap of thirty-one impurities.

By way of elements:

  1. These hairs of the head, being a part of the body: are a portion of the earth element (paṭhavī-dhātu), which is not a living being, not a person, not a self, and has no owner.
  2. These hairs of the body, being a part of the body: are a portion of the earth element (paṭhavī-dhātu), which is not a living being, not a person, not a self, and has no owner.
  3. These nails, being a part of the body: are a portion of the earth element (paṭhavī-dhātu), which is not a living being, not a person, not a self, and has no owner.
  4. These teeth, being a part of the body: are a portion of the earth element (paṭhavī-dhātu), which is not a living being, not a person, not a self, and has no owner.
  5. This skin, being a part of the body: is a portion of the earth element (paṭhavī-dhātu), which is not a living being, not a person, not a self, and has no owner.

The candidate for ordination should continue this meditation without neglect even after going forth.

After the hair is shaved, the candidate must bathe and cleanse himself thoroughly with soap to remove the scent of the laity. If the candidate is unable to do so himself, you, as the teacher, must bathe and cleanse the candidate for going forth (pabbajjāpekkha). In doing so, act out of compassion, without feeling disgust, just as a father would bathe a beloved son. Thereafter, having dressed him in clean clothes, lead the candidate to the place of ordination. Upon arrival, if the parents of the clansman (kulaputta) are present, he should first pay homage to their feet and take leave of them. Then, having paid homage to the feet of the preceptor (upajjhāya) and the assembled bhikkhus, he should take the robes in his hands, sit in the squatting position (ukkutika) before the preceptor, and with hands in añjali, request the going forth. The formula for this is as follows:

Okāsa! Ahaṃ bhante! Pabbajjaṃ yācāmi.

Dutiyampi ahaṃ bhante! Pabbajjaṃ yācāmi.

Tatiyampi ahaṃ bhante! Pabbajjaṃ yācāmi.

Sabba dukkha nissaraṇa nibbāna sacchikaraṇatthāya imaṃ kāsāvaṃ gahetvā pabbājetha maṃ bhante! Anukampaṃ upādāya.

Dutiyampi sabba dukkha nissaraṇa nibbāna sacchikaraṇatthāya imaṃ kāsāvaṃ gahetvā pabbājetha maṃ bhante! Anukampaṃ upādāya.

Tatiyampi sabba dukkha nissaraṇa nibbāna sacchikaraṇatthāya imaṃ kāsāvaṃ gahetvā pabbājetha maṃ bhante! Anukampaṃ upādāya.

Having recited this formula and offered his robes into the hands of the preceptor, he should again sit in the squatting position before the preceptor and, with hands in añjali, recite this formula:

Sabba dukkha nissaraṇa nibbāna sacchikaraṇatthāya etaṃ kāsāvaṃ datvā pabbājetha maṃ bhante anukampaṃ upādāya. Dutiyampi sabba dukkha -pe- upādāya. Tatiyampi sabba dukkha -pe- upādāya.

When the candidate has requested the robes with this formula, you, as the teacher, should place the shoulder-strap (aṃsa) around the candidate's neck and give him the robes you hold for the purpose of dressing and robing. The candidate should then reflect upon the robes received from the teacher and put them on. If the candidate is unable to dress himself, the teacher or another should assist him. One should not put on the robes as a layman without receiving them from a teacher. If the candidate is already wearing robes, they must be removed and then given back to him for the ordination. If the Refuges are undertaken without doing so, the going forth is not established. It is also permissible for another to dress him upon the preceptor's command. If the preceptor gives the robes to a layman saying, "Dress him in these robes," it is permissible for the layman to do so by the preceptor's command. After being robed, the candidate should return to the preceptor, pay homage to his feet with the fivefold prostration, sit in the squatting position with hands in añjali, and request the ten precepts of going forth along with the Triple Refuge using this formula:

Okāsa! Ahaṃ bhante tisaraṇena saddhiṃ pabbajjā dasasīlaṃ dhammaṃ yācāmi: anuggahaṃ katvā sīlaṃ detha me bhante. Dutiyampi ahaṃ bhante -pe- me bhante. Tatiyampi ahaṃ bhante -pe- me bhante anukampaṃ upādāya.

When this has been said, the preceptor should say, “Yamahaṃ vadāmi taṃ vadehi” and have him recite the Namaskāra, and then:

Buddhaṃ saraṇaṃ gacchāmi.

Dhammaṃ saraṇaṃ gacchāmi.

Saṅghaṃ saraṇaṃ gacchāmi.

.

Dutiyampi Buddhaṃ saraṇaṃ gacchāmi.

Dutiyampi Dhammaṃ saraṇaṃ gacchāmi.

Dutiyampi Saṅghaṃ saraṇaṃ gacchāmi.

.

Tatiyampi Buddhaṃ saraṇaṃ gacchāmi.

Tatiyampi Dhammaṃ saraṇaṃ gacchāmi.

Tatiyampi Saṅghaṃ saraṇaṃ gacchāmi.

The Refuges should be undertaken either by joining the words with the niggahita ending (e.g., “Buddhaṃ saraṇaṃ gacchāmi”) or by separating the words with the 'm' ending (e.g., “Buddham saraṇam gacchāmi”). It is better to have them undertaken in both ways. Many teachers do so. Even if the training rules such as “Pāṇātipātā veramaṇī sikkhāpadaṃ samādiyāmi” are not recited, the status of a novice (sāmaṇera) is attained through the undertaking of the Refuges alone. The recitation of the training rules is not the primary factor here.

Since the intention (cetanā) is the primary factor in a layman's taking of refuge, the taking of refuge occurs regardless of the language or manner in which the training rules are recited. However, for a novice, the taking of refuge occurs only when the teacher recites the words in the Pali language without error in pronunciation or articulation (ṭhāna-karaṇa-sampatti), and the pupil likewise recites them without error. If even one of the two—teacher or pupil—is unable to pronounce correctly, the taking of refuge does not occur. Elderly persons without teeth cannot pronounce them perfectly. Therefore, when an elderly thera without teeth ordains a pupil, he should have another person administer the Refuges.

Taking a Preceptor

After undertaking the novice virtue, the one who has received the going-forth should pay homage to the Preceptor, sit in the squatting position, and take the Preceptor by reciting three times the phrase: “Upajjhāyo me bhante hohi”. When the pupil has spoken thus, the Preceptor should accept it by saying: “Pāsādikena sampādehi”.

According to the word of the Buddha: “Upajjhāyo bhikkhave! saddhivihārikamhi putta cittaṃ upaṭṭhapessati. Saddhivihāriko upajjhāyamhi pitūcittaṃ upaṭṭhapessati. Evaṃ te aññamaññaṃ sagāravā sappatissā sabhāgavuttikā viharantā imasmiṃ dhammavinaye vuddhiṃ virūḷhiṃ vepullaṃ āpajjanti”, the novice should regard the Preceptor as his father, and with great respect and reverence for the Preceptor, live in complete obedience to him. The Preceptor should also regard his co-resident novice as a son and be compassionate toward him. The Preceptor should train the new novice in wearing the robes and carrying the bowl, etc., by having him reside in his presence. Until these are mastered, he should protect the new novice like one protects an infant, without sending him on invitations and such. He should teach the training rules that a novice must observe. The novice also—

“Anujānāmi bhikkhave! dasabhaṅgehi samannāgataṃ sāmaṇeraṃ nāsetuṃ, pāṇātipāti hoti. Adinnādāyi hoti, ab‍rahmacārī hoti. Musāvādi hoti, majjapāyi hoti, buddhassa avaṇṇaṃ bhāsati, dhammassa avaṇṇaṃ bhāsati, saṅghassa avaṇṇaṃ bhāsati, micchādiṭṭhiko hoti, bhikkhunī dusako hoti”

—should live obediently without breaking even one of these ten factors for expulsion mentioned above. If even one of these is violated, the entire novice virtue, beginning from the going for refuge, is broken. The taking of the Preceptor is also nullified. He loses his right to the lodging he received in the monastery and to the gains of the Community. If he does not establish himself in future restraint and take the refuges again, that novice should be expelled by having his robes removed and being driven out.

If a novice who has committed a defeat acknowledges his fault and wishes to be established in restraint again, he should be made to take the refuges again and take a Preceptor again. The going for refuge of novices is like the formal act of Higher Ordination (upasampadā-kammavācā) for bhikkhus. Just as the undertaking of the fourfold purification of virtue occurs for a bhikkhu through the recitation of the ordination kammavācā, the undertaking of the ten precepts for novices occurs through the going for refuge itself. However, for the sake of establishing future restraint and for the sake of firming the commitment, after the going for refuge, he should also be made to undertake the ten training rules. If a novice commits a fault that does not belong to the ten factors for expulsion, some form of punishment such as the water-punishment or sand-punishment should be imposed. By undergoing the punishment and being established in future restraint, his virtue is purified. There is no fault in not undertaking the training rules again.

Those Unfit for Going-forth

There are two classes of persons unfit for Going-forth: those who are excluded from Going-forth such that even if the ceremony is performed, the status is not attained; and those for whom Going-forth is prohibited because it is unseemly, though the status would be attained if the ceremony were performed. A teacher incurs an offense of wrongdoing (dukkaṭa) by granting Going-forth to those who are unfit.

There are eleven persons excluded from Going-forth:

  1. Paṇḍako bhikkhave! anupasampanno na upasampādetabbo, upasampanno nāsetabbo.
  2. Ubhatobyañjanako bhikkhave! anupasampanno na upasampādetabbo, upasampanno nāsetabbo.
  3. Theyyasaṃvāsako bhikkhave! anupasampanno na upasampādetabbo, upasampanno nāsetabbo.
  4. Titthiyapakkantako bhikkhave! anupasampanno na upasampādetabbo, upasampanno nāsetabbo.
  5. Tiracchānagato bhikkhave! anupasampanno na upasampādetabbo, upasampanno nāsetabbo.
  6. Mātughātako bhikkhave! anupasampanno na upasampādetabbo, upasampanno nāsetabbo.
  7. Pitughātako bhikkhave! anupasampanno na upasampādetabbo, upasampanno nāsetabbo.
  8. Arahantaghātako bhikkhave! anupasampanno na upasampādetabbo, upasampanno nāsetabbo.
  9. Lohituppādako bhikkhave! anupasampanno na upasampādetabbo, upasampanno nāsetabbo.
  10. Saṅghabhedako bhikkhave! anupasampanno na upasampādetabbo, upasampanno nāsetabbo.
  11. Bhikkhunīdūsako bhikkhave! anupasampanno na upasampādetabbo, upasampanno nāsetabbo.

The eleven persons mentioned in these eleven disciplinary rules—the paṇḍaka, ubhatobyañjanaka, theyyasaṃvāsaka, titthiyapakkantaka, tiracchānagata, mātughātaka, pitughātaka, arahantaghātaka, lohituppādaka, saṅghabhedaka, and bhikkhunīdūsaka—are excluded from both Going-forth and Higher Ordination. Even if they are given the Going-forth, the status is not established. Even if they are given the Higher Ordination, the status is not established.

Paṇḍaka refers to persons with a deficiency in gender characteristics. There are five types of paṇḍaka: the āsittapaṇḍaka, the usūyapaṇḍaka, the opakkamikapaṇḍaka, the pakkhapaṇḍaka, and the napuṃsakapaṇḍaka. One who calms his sexual fever by taking another's genitals in his mouth and sucking the impurity is called an āsittapaṇḍaka. One who calms his sexual fever by watching others engage in sexual intercourse is called an usūyapaṇḍaka. One who has been castrated, as is done to dogs or cattle, is called an opakkamikapaṇḍaka. One who, through the power of unwholesome kamma, becomes a paṇḍaka during the waning half of the month is called a pakkhapaṇḍaka. One who is born without either male or female gender characteristics is called a napuṃsakapaṇḍaka. Among these five types of paṇḍaka, Going-forth is not prohibited for the āsitta and usūya paṇḍaka. The remaining three are unfit for Going-forth. Even for the pakkhapaṇḍaka, Going-forth is prohibited only during the period he remains a paṇḍaka. During that time, he becomes crazed with sexual fever. The sexual fever of the āsitta and usūya paṇḍaka can be calmed through wise reflection (yoniso manasikāra) and other means. The sexual fever of the pakkhapaṇḍaka cannot be so calmed. That is why he is unfit for Going-forth during that period.

Ubhatobyañjanaka refers to a person in whom both male and female characteristics are present. There are two types: the female ubhatobyañjanaka and the male ubhatobyañjanaka. Among them, the female ubhatobyañjanaka is capable of both conceiving and causing other women to conceive. The male ubhatobyañjanaka can cause women to conceive but cannot conceive himself. Both are capable of performing both male and female roles during sexual gratification.

There are three types of theyyasaṃvāsaka: the liṅgatthenaka, the saṃvāsatthenaka, and the ubhayatthenaka. One who, without receiving Going-forth from a teacher, dons the robes himself, does not declare his seniority in rains, does not accept salutation according to seniority, does not obstruct other bhikkhus by taking a seat, and does not participate in formal acts of the Community (vinaya kamma), but merely steals the outward appearance of a recluse, is called a liṅgatthenaka. One who has received only the novice Going-forth from bhikkhus but goes to another region and falsely declares, "I have this many rains," accepts salutations from bhikkhus according to seniority, obstructs bhikkhus by taking a seat, and presents himself at formal acts of the Community, is called a saṃvāsatthenaka because he steals only the communion. One who dons the robes himself, goes to a monastery, declares a number of rains, accepts salutations from bhikkhus, obstructs bhikkhus by taking a seat, and participates in formal acts of the Community, is called an ubhayatthenaka. A detailed explanation of the theyyasaṃvāsaka is found in the Samantapāsādikā, which should be consulted.

Titthiyapakkantaka refers to one who, having been ordained as a bhikkhu in the Buddha's Dispensation, later takes up the going-forth of another sect and adopts their views.

Tiracchānagata here refers not only to animals but to all non-human beings such as nāga, supaṇṇa, deva, and brahmā. Going-forth is a privilege reserved for human beings alone.

Mātughātaka refers to one who has committed the act of matricide, which is one of the weightier kammas with immediate result (ānantariya kamma). This kamma occurs when a child of the human race intentionally takes the life of a mother who is also of the human race.

The same applies to the pitughātaka (patricide). Even if one does not know that the person being killed is one's father, the ānantariya kamma is committed by the act of killing. Details should be consulted in the Samantapāsādikā. An explanation of these kammas is also found in the 'Handbook of the Buddhist'.

Arahantaghātaka should also be understood in terms of human Arahants. If a human intentionally takes the life of an Arahant of the human race, whether ordained or not, it is an ānantariya kamma. He cannot receive Going-forth. If one kills an Arahant who is not human, it is only a grave sin, not an ānantariya kamma. If an animal takes the life of a human Arahant, the ānantariya kamma does not occur; it is only a grave sin.

Lohituppādaka refers to one who, like Devadatta, with a malicious mind, causes even a single drop of blood to be shed from the body of a living Buddha. For him, Going-forth and Higher Ordination are prohibited. For one who draws blood from the Buddha's body with a mind of loving-kindness in order to cure an illness, there is merit and no sin.

Saṅghabhedaka refers to one who, like Devadatta, causes a schism in the Community by means of any of the four types of formal acts. This is a kamma that can only be committed by one who has received Higher Ordination, not by one who has not.

Bhikkhunīdūsaka refers to one who has violated a regular bhikkhuni in any of the three passages. For him, Going-forth and Higher Ordination are prohibited. For one who has merely compromised the virtue of a bhikkhuni through bodily contact, Going-forth and Higher Ordination are not prohibited.

Persons who should not be ordained, but whose ordination is valid if performed.

“Na bhikkhave! ananuññāto mātā pitūhi putto pabbājetabbo. Yo pabbājey‍ya āpatti dukkaṭassa” Since this is a Vinaya regulation, do not ordain a son who has not been permitted by his mother and father. Even if one who was previously ordained with parental permission has left the robes and returns to be ordained again, do not ordain him without obtaining parental permission once more. If parents have entrusted a child saying, “I entrust him to you; ordain him whenever you wish,” then you may ordain that child whenever he comes, even if he has left the robes many times, without further consulting the parents. If someone comes to the monastery after a quarrel with his parents and asks for ordination, and when told to bring parental permission, says, “I will not go to my parents; if you do not ordain me, I will take my life or cause harm to you,” then ordain him for the sake of his protection. If his parents come and inquire about such a person who has been ordained, inform them of the circumstances.

“Na bhikkhave! pañcahi ābādhehi puṭṭho pabbājetabbo. Yo pabbājey‍ya āpatti dukkaṭassa” Since this is a Vinaya regulation, do not ordain anyone afflicted with any of the five diseases. The five diseases are those known as Kuṭṭha, gaṇḍa, kilāsa, sosa, apamāra.

‘Kuṭṭha’ (Leprosy) refers to diseases arising on the skin such as red spots, black spots, and scabs. Do not ordain one who has leprosy even the size of a little finger-nail if it is in the spreading stage. It is suitable to ordain one who has a small patch of leprosy only the size of a nail on a part of the body covered by the robe, provided it is in the healing stage. When ordaining a leper after treatment, ordain him only when the skin has returned to its natural color. The Vinaya Commentary states that one whose body is covered in ash-like scales like the back of a monitor lizard should also not be ordained.

‘Gaṇḍa’ (Boils) refers to unripened lumps arising here and there on the body like ebony fruits or limes. Some call these cystic tumors. Do not ordain one who has a growing boil even the size of a jujube fruit. One who has a non-growing boil the size of a jujube fruit in a covered area may be ordained. Do not ordain one who has non-growing boils on uncovered areas like the face. When ordaining after treating boils, ordain only after the skin has returned to normal. Do not ordain while the wound is still present.

‘Kilāsa’ (Eczema/Dry Leprosy) is a specific type of dry skin disease appearing in the color of red or white lotus petals. The condition commonly known today as vitiligo is Kilāsa. Regarding those with this disease, follow the same procedure as mentioned for leprosy.

‘Sosa’ (Consumption) is tuberculosis, which gradually wastes and dries the body day by day, leading the patient to death.

‘Apamāra’ (Epilepsy) is the falling sickness where a person suddenly loses consciousness and falls to the ground, sometimes struggling and foaming at the mouth with phlegm and blood. It is said to be a disease caused by non-human spirits.

“Na bhikkhave! rājabhaṭo pabbājetabbo. Yo pabbājey‍ya, āpatti dukkaṭassa” Since this is a Vinaya regulation, do not ordain a king’s soldier (government servant). A ‘king’s soldier’ is one who receives a salary from a king or a government for performing any task. It is proper to ordain such persons after they have resigned from their service.

“Na bhikkhave! dhajabandho coro pabbājetabbo. Yo pabbājey‍ya āpatti dukkaṭassa” As declared, do not ordain a notorious thief who commits serious robberies. One who was previously a notorious thief but has since abandoned that path and entered the right way, and is known as such by the public, is suitable for ordination. One who has committed minor thefts but is not publicly known as a thief may be given ordination.

“Na bhikkhave! kārabhedako coro pabbājetabbo. Yo pabbājey‍ya āpatti dukkaṭassa” Since these are Vinaya rules, do not ordain a thief who has escaped from confinement. This includes one who has escaped from a prison, one who has escaped from guards while being moved from place to place, and one who has fled an area after being restricted to it; all such persons are unfit for ordination. If one who was living righteously was imprisoned due to the slander of others and escaped, do not ordain him in that same region. He may be ordained in another region.

“Na bhikkhave! likhitako coro pabbājetabbo. Yo pabbājey‍ya āpatti dukkaṭassa” Since these are Vinaya rules, do not ordain a thief whose name is recorded by the government for arrest or punishment.

“Na bhikkhave! kasāhato katadaṇḍakammo pabbājetabbo. Yo pabbājey‍ya āpatti dukkaṭassa” Since these are Vinaya rules, do not ordain one who has been flogged with a whip or other instrument as punishment. One who was beaten for failing to follow instructions may be ordained. Those who should not be ordained are those flogged as punishment for non-payment of taxes, non-payment of debts, etc. Even such a person is fit to receive ordination after the wounds have healed.

“Na bhikkhave! lakkhaṇāhato katadaṇḍakammo pabbājetabbo. Yo pabbājey‍ya āpatti dukkaṭassa” Since these are Vinaya rules, do not ordain one who has been branded or marked on the body as punishment. If the branded person is a free man, he may be ordained after the wounds have healed and the scars have faded. One who has scars in places not visible when the robes are worn covering the three circles may also be ordained.

“Na bhikkhave! iṇāyiko pabbājetabbo. Yo pabbājey‍ya āpatti dukkaṭassa” As declared, do not ordain debtors. If a relative or a lay supporter of the person seeking ordination undertakes to pay the debt, he may be ordained. If the preceptor has funds to pay the debt, he may ordain the debtor with the intention of freeing him by paying the creditors. Do not ordain with the thought of finding money to pay the debt later. There is no offense for the preceptor if he ordains a person without knowing he is a debtor.

“Na bhikkhave! dāso pabbājetabbo. Yo pabbājey‍ya āpatti dukkaṭassa” As declared, do not ordain a slave. Since there are no slaves at present, no detailed explanation is provided here. There is an extensive explanation regarding this in the Commentary.

Thirty-two types of persons who should not be ordained.

“Na bhikkhave! hatthacchinno pabbājetabbo. Na pādacchinno pabbājetabbo. Na hatthapādacchinno pabbājetabbo. Na kaṇṇacchinno pabbājetabbo. Na kāsacchinno pabbājetabbo. Nakaṇṇanāsacchinno pabbājetabbo. Na aṅgulicchinno pabbājetabbo. Na alacchinno pabbājetabbo. Na kaṇḍaracchinno pabbājetabbo. Na phaṇahattho pabbājetabbo. Na khujjo pabbājetabbo. Na vāmano pabbājetabbo. Na galagaṇḍi pabbājetabbo. Na lakkhaṇāhato pabbājetabbo. Na kasāhato pabbājetabbo. Na likhitako pabbājetabbo. Na sīpadi pabbājetabbo. Na pāparogi pabbājetabbo. Na parisadūsako pabbājetabbo. Na kāṇo pabbājetabbo. Na kuṇi pabbājetabbo. Na bañjo pabbājetabbo. Na pakkhahato pabbājetabbo. Na chinniriyāpatho pabbājetabbo. Na jarādubbalo pabbājetabbo. Na andho pabbājetabbo. Na mūgo pabbājetabbo. Na badhiro pabbājetabbo. Na andhamūgo pabbājetabbo. Na andhabadhiro pabbājetabbo. Na mugabadhiro pabbājetabbo. Na andhamugabadhiro pabbājetabbo. Yo pabbājey‍ya āpatti dukkaṭassa.

As the Tathāgata has declared in the Vinaya, the following should not be ordained: one whose hands are cut off, one whose feet are cut off, one whose hands and feet are cut off, one whose ears are cut off, one whose nose is cut off, one whose ears and nose are cut off, one whose thumbs are cut off, one whose little fingers are cut off, one with severed tendons, one with webbed fingers, a hunchback, a dwarf, one with a goiter, one with branded marks on the body, one with marks of flogging, one who is a wanted criminal, one with elephantiasis, one with a bad disease, one who disfigures the assembly, one who is one-eyed, one with a crooked limb, one who is lame, one who is paralyzed on one side, a cripple, one feeble with age, one who is blind, one who is dumb, one who is deaf, one who is blind and dumb, one who is blind and deaf, one who is dumb and deaf, and one who is blind, dumb, and deaf. If ordained, there is an offense of wrong-doing for the preceptor.

One whose finger-tips only are cut off such that even a small portion of the nail remains may be ordained. If even one finger is cut off such that no nail remains, he should not be ordained.

‘One with severed tendons’ refers to one who, due to severed tendons, cannot place the entire sole of the foot on the ground and walks only on the tips of the toes or only on the heels.

‘One with webbed fingers’ refers to one whose fingers are joined together.

‘One with a bad disease’ refers to one suffering from diseases such as asthma, hemorrhoids, fistula, or chronic cough, which are loathed by the public.

‘One who disfigures the assembly’ refers to one who, due to deformity, makes the assembly look unsightly. This includes: extremely tall persons whose heads reach the waist of others; extremely short persons like monstrous images; extremely dark persons like a charred log; extremely pale persons like a plastered wall; extremely thin persons like hungry ghosts (petas) with only skin and bone; extremely obese persons who find it difficult to move; those with massive bellies like goblins (pisacas); and those with heads so large they cannot be held up. A detailed description of those who disfigure the assembly is found in the Vinaya Commentary, where many such types are listed.

The Age for Ordination

“Na bhikkhave! ūnapaṇṇarasavasso dārako pabbājetabbo. Yo pabbājey‍ya āpatti dukkaṭassa.” Regarding this, the Blessed One once declared that a boy under fifteen years of age should not be given the Going Forth, and that whoever should give the Going Forth to such a one commits an offense of wrong-doing.

In later times, a very devout family who were attendants to the Venerable Ānanda Thera passed away due to the plague (ahivātaka disease). Two young children of that family survived. When they saw the bhikkhus, they would run towards them out of past familiarity. The bhikkhus would drive them away. Receiving no compassion from the bhikkhus, those children would cry. The heart of the Venerable Ānanda Thera was moved by their suffering, and thinking, “The Buddha has laid down a training rule that children under fifteen years of age should not be ordained; what can be done so that these children do not perish?” he reported the matter to the Buddha.

Then the Tathāgata asked, “Ānanda, are those children capable of scaring away crows?” When he replied, “Yes, Venerable Sir,” the Buddha addressed the bhikkhus and said: “Anujānāmi bhikkhave! ūnapaṇṇarasavassaṃ kākuḍḍepakaṃ pabbājetujaṃ”—thus allowing the Going Forth for children who have not reached fifteen years of age but are capable of scaring away crows. Although the Buddha allowed the ordination of children capable of scaring away crows to prevent those two boys from perishing in that emergency, it should be understood as the Buddha's intention that children under fifteen years of age are generally unsuitable for the Going Forth. Therefore, except for a special reason, children under fifteen years of age should not be ordained. The period between fifteen and twenty years of age should be considered the most suitable time for ordaining young men. For those who ordain late in life, it is difficult to discipline the mind and body to be suitable for the life of a renunciant. Therefore, the Buddha declared:

“Pañcahi dhammehi samannāgato dullabho vuḍḍhapabbajito. Katamehi pañcahi? Dullabho bhikkhave! buḍḍhapabbajito suvaco. Dullabho suggahitagāhī. Dullabho padakkhiṇaggāhī. Dullabho dhammakathiko. Dullabho vinayadharo.”

This means that a late-life ordinee possessed of five qualities is rare. “Bhikkhus, a late-life ordinee possessed of these five qualities is rare. Which five? Bhikkhus, a late-life ordinee who is easy to speak to is rare; a late-life ordinee who learns well from teachers is rare; a late-life ordinee who accepts the instructions of teachers respectfully is rare; a late-life ordinee who is a speaker of Dhamma is rare; a late-life ordinee who is a bearer of the Vinaya is rare.” In another Sutta, the Buddha also declared: “Dullabho ākappasampanno”—that a late-life ordinee accomplished in proper conduct and deportment is rare.

Wealth, education, or noble birth do not appear in the Buddha's teaching as factors to be considered when determining suitability or unsuitability for ordination. If one is free from the aforementioned disqualifications for the Going Forth, anyone—whether rich or poor, educated or uneducated—is suitable for ordination.

“Yebhuy‍yena hi kattiyakulato pabbajitā jātiṃ nissāya mānaṃ karonti. B‍rāhmaṇakulā pabbajitā mante nissāya mānaṃ karonti. Hīnajaccakulā pabbajitā attano vijātitāya patiṭṭhātuṃ na sakkonti. Gahapatidārakā pana kacchehi sedaṃ muñcantehi piṭṭhiyā loṇaṃ pupphamānāya bhūmiṃ kasitvā nihatamānadappā honti. Te pabbajitvā mānaṃ vā dappaṃ vā akatvā buddhavacanaṃ uggahetvā vipassanāya kammaṃ karontā sakkonti arahatte patiṭṭhātuṃ.”

This passage from the Commentary to the Cūḷahatthipadopama Sutta should also be considered in this regard.

The Procedure for Higher Ordination.

The procedure for Higher Ordination is a grave Monastic Procedure in the Buddha's Dispensation. Unlike the act of going forth (pabbajja), it cannot be performed everywhere; it must be performed only within a consecrated boundary (sima). If it is performed in the Middle Country, it must be carried out by a Community of ten or more than ten bhikkhus, as stated: “Anujānāmi bhikkhave! dasavaggena vā atireka dasa vaggena vā gaṇena upasampādetuṃ”. If it is performed in the border districts, it may be carried out by a group of five bhikkhus with a Vinaya-expert as the fifth, as stated: “Anujānāmi bhikkhave! sabbapaccantimesu janapadesu vinayadharapañcamena gaṇena upasampādetuṃ”. The Blessed One Himself has designated the border districts.

They are as follows:-

“Puratthimāya disāya kajaṅgalaṃ nāma nigamo, tassā parena mahāsālā, tato’parā paccantimā janapadā, orato majjhe. Puratthimadakkhiṇāya disāya salalavatī nāma nadī, tato ‘Parā paccantimā janapadā, orato majjhe. Dakkhiṇāya disāya setakaṇṇikaṃ nāma nigamo, tato” parā paccantimā janapadā orato majjhe. Pacchimāya disāya thūnaṃ nāma b‍rāhmaṇa gāmo, tato’ parā paccantimā janapadā orato majjhe. Uttarāya disāya usīraddhajo nāma pabbato, tato’parā paccantimā janapadā orato majjhe.

Speaking from the perspective of the Middle Country: to the east, beyond the market town of Kajangala; to the south-east, beyond the river Salalavati; to the south, beyond the market town of Setakannika; to the west, beyond the brahmin village of Thuna; and to the north, beyond the mountain Usiraddhaja—these are the border districts. Since our country also belongs to the border districts, the procedure for Higher Ordination can be performed here with five bhikkhus. In performing a Higher Ordination, the majority of the duties fall upon the Act-proclaimer (kammacariya). When the candidate for Higher Ordination arrives at the boundary, if it is difficult to recite the Act-proclamation using the original names of the candidate and the Preceptor, the name Nāga should be assigned to the candidate and Tissa to the Preceptor. This authorization should be made by the Act-proclaimer as follows:

Discarding the former name of this candidate for Higher Ordination, I assign to him the name Nāga; and discarding the former name of the candidate's Preceptor, I assign to him the name Tissa for the ease of reciting the Act-proclamation. This should be known by the candidate for Higher Ordination, by the Preceptor, and by the Great Community.

After the authorization of names, the Act-proclaimer should speak to the candidate for Higher Ordination as follows:

Since the Protector of the World has stated, “Paṭhamaṃ upajjhaṃ gāhāpetabbo”: "O novice, who has come to receive Higher Ordination with the desire to cross the ocean of samsara and reach the city of Nibbana! You must first take a Preceptor."

When this is said, the candidate for Higher Ordination should sit in the squatting posture before the Preceptor and say the following sentence three times: “Upajjhāyo me bhante hohi”. The Preceptor should accept it by saying, “Pāsādikena sampādehi”.

Thereafter, the Act-proclaimer should speak as follows: "Venerable novice! Since the Blessed One, the Lord of the World, has stated, “Upajjhāṃ gāhāpetvā pattacīvaraṃ ācikkhitabbaṃ”, I shall now speak to you so that you may know the monastic designations regarding the bowl and robes. “Ayaṃ te patto”: Venerable novice, this black-colored, circular vessel is your bowl. “Ayaṃ saṃghāṭi”: This robe upon your shoulder, made of cut pieces, sewn double-layered, and dyed, is known in monastic usage as the outer robe (sanghati). “Ayaṃ uttarāsaṃgo”: This robe upon your body, made of cut pieces, sewn single-layered, and dyed, is known in monastic usage as the upper robe (uttarasanga). “Ayaṃ antaravāsako”: This robe which you are wearing is known in monastic usage as the inner robe (antaravasaka).

‘Gaccha: amumhi okāse tiṭṭhāhi. Venerable novice! Since the Tathagata has stated, “Anujānāmi bhikkhave! ekamantaṃ anusāsitvā saṅgha majjhe antarāyike dhamme pucchituṃ”—that the candidate for Higher Ordination should be instructed in a place apart from the Community, and since it is inappropriate to instruct you in the midst of the Community—go and stand in that place over there."

When this is said, the candidate for Higher Ordination should rise and, without turning his back to the Great Community, walk a short distance with hands joined in reverence and stand there. The Act-proclaimer should go there and instruct the candidate for Higher Ordination. Since it is stated, “Na bhikkhave! asammatena anusāsitabbo. Yo anusāsey‍ya āpatti dukkaṭassa”, one should not go to instruct without obtaining authorization. Now, the Act-proclaimer should authorize himself by reciting the following sentence:

Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho, nāgo āyasmato tissassa upasampadāpekkho. Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ ahaṃ nāgaṃ anusāsey‍yaṃ.

Thereafter, rising from the midst of the Community and approaching the candidate for Higher Ordination, he should instruct him while standing as follows:

Suṇasi nāga! aya te saccakālo, bhūtakālo, yaṃ jātaṃ taṃ saṅghamajjhe pucchante santaṃ atthiti vattabbaṃ. Asantaṃ natthiti vattabbaṃ. Mā kho vit‍thāsi, mā kho maṅku ahosi.

"O novice named Naga, who has dedicated your life to the Buddha's Dispensation to cross the ocean of samsara! This is the time for you to receive the supreme Higher Ordination morality, characterized by higher virtue in this most rare Dispensation of the All-Knowing One. This is the time to speak only the truth honestly, without hiding anything. This is the time to declare things as they are, without attempting to conceal the facts. If there is any disease such as leprosy or boils on your body, when you are questioned in the midst of the Community, you must say 'It is' if it exists. You must say 'It is not' if it does not exist. When questioned in the midst of the Community, do not be terrified with the fear that they are questioning you to do you harm. Do not be displeased or frown, thinking they are questioning whether non-existent things exist in order to disparage you."

“Evaṃ taṃ pucchissanti, santi te evarūpā ābādhā, kuṭṭhaṃ, gaṇḍo, kilāso, soso, apamāro, manussosi, purisosi, bhujissosi, anaṇosi, nasi rājabhaṭo, anuññātosi mātā pitūhi, paripuṇṇavīsativassosi, paripuṇṇaṃ te pattacīvaraṃ, kiṃ nāmosi, ko nāmo te upajjhāyoti.”

"Venerable novice! Since the Tathagata has allowed the questioning of the thirteen obstructive factors from the candidate for Higher Ordination, stating, “Anujānāmi bhikkhave! upasampādentena terasa antarāyike dhamme pucchituṃ”, they will question you in the midst of the Community during this preliminary stage of the Higher Ordination as follows: Do you have any of the diseases known as leprosy, boils, eczema, consumption, or epilepsy? Are you a human being? Are you a man? Are you a free man? Are you without debt? Are you not in royal service? Have you been permitted by your parents? Are you a full twenty years of age? Are your bowl and robes complete? What is your name? What is your Preceptor's name? Thus they will question you in the midst of the Community."

Having instructed him thus, leave the candidate for Higher Ordination standing there, and the Act-proclaimer should return to the midst of the Community and say:

“Suṇātu me bhante saṃgho, nāgo āyasmato tissassa upasampadāpekkho anusiṭṭho so mayā yadi saṃghassa pattallaṃ nāgo āgacchey‍ya”

Having said this sentence and obtained permission from the Community, summon the candidate for Higher Ordination to the midst of the Community. Thereafter, since it is stated, “Na bhikkhave! ayācitena upasampādetabbo. Yo upasampādey‍ya āpatti dukkaṭassa”, have the candidate for Higher Ordination request the Higher Ordination. At this point, the candidate for Higher Ordination should wear the robe over one shoulder, pay homage at the feet of the bhikkhus, sit in the squatting posture with hands joined in reverence, and say:

“Saṃghaṃ bhante upasampadaṃ yācāmi, ullumpatu maṃ bhante saṅgho anukampaṃ upādāya. Dutiyampi bhante saṅghaṃ upasampadaṃ yācāmi, ullumpatu maṃ bhante saṅgho anukampaṃ upādāya. Tatiyampi bhante saṅghaṃ upasampadaṃ yācāmi, ullumpatu maṃ bhaṇte saṅgho anukampaṃ upādāya.” Thus he should request Higher Ordination.

Then, the Act-proclaimer, having obtained authorization to question the obstructive factors through the formal act: “Suṇātu me bhante saṅgho, ayaṃ nāgo āyasmato tissassa upasampadāpekkho yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ ahaṃ nāgaṃ antarāyike dhamme pucchey‍yaṃ”, should question the obstructive factors as follows:

Suṇasi nāga! ayaṃ te saccakālo, bhūtakālo, yaṃ jātaṃ taṃ pucchāmi. Santaṃ atthiti vattabbaṃ. Asantaṃ natthiti vattabbaṃ. Santi te evarūpā ābādhā kuṭṭhaṃ? (natthi bhante) Gaṇḍo? (natthi bhante) Kilāso? (natthi bhante) Soso? (natthi bhante) Apamāro? (natthi bhante) Manussosi? (āma bhante) Purisosi? (āma bhante) Bhujissosi? (āma bhante) Anaṇosi? (āma bhante) Nasi rājābhaṭo? (āma bhante) Anuññātosi mātāpituhi? (āma bhante) Paripuṇṇa vīsati vassosi? (āma bhante) Paripuṇṇaṃ te pattacīvaraṃ? (āma bhante) Kiṃ nāmosi? (Ahaṃ bhante nāgo nāma) Ko nāmo te upajjhāyo? (Upajjhāyo me bhante āyasmā tissatthero nāma) After questioning the obstructive factors, the Higher Ordination should be performed using the Act-proclamation consisting of a motion and three announcements (natticatuttha-kamma).

The Formal Act for Higher Ordination

“Suṇātu me bhante saṅgho. Ayaṃ nāgo āyasmato tissassa upasampadāpekkho parisuddho antarāyikehi dhammehi, paripuṇṇassa pattacīvaraṃ. Nāgo saṅghaṃ upasampadaṃ yācati, āyasmatā tissena upajjhāyena, yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ. Saṅgho nāgaṃ upasampādey‍ya, āyasmatā tissena upajjhāyena. Esā ñatti.

Suṇātu me bhante saṅgho, ayaṃ nāgo āyasmato tissassa upasampadāpekkho, parisuddho antarāyike hi dhammehi, paripuṇṇassa pattacīvaraṃ, nāgo saṅghaṃ upasampadaṃ yācati, āyasmatā tissena upajjhāyena. Saṅgho nāgaṃ upasampādeti. Āyasmatā tissena upajjhāyena. Yassāyasmato khamati nāgassa upasampadā āyasmatā tissena upajjhāyena. So tuṇhassa. Yassa nakkhamati so bhāsey‍ya.

Dutiyampi etamat‍thaṃ vadāmi. Suṇātu me bhante saṅgho. Ayaṃ nāgo āyasmato tissassa upasampadāpekkho, parisuddho antarāyike hi dhammehi. Paripuṇṇassa pattacīvaraṃ. Nāge saṅghaṃ upasampadaṃ yācati. Āyasmatā tissena upajjhāyena. Saṅgho nāgaṃ upasampādeti. Āyasmatā tissena upajjhāyena. Yassāyasmato khamati nāgassa upasampadā, āyasmatā tissena upajjhāyena. So tuṇhassa. Yassa nakkhamati so bhāsey‍ya.

Tatiyampi etamat‍thaṃ vadāmi. Suṇātu me bhante saṅgho. Ayaṃ nāgo āyasmato tissassa upasampadāpekkho. Parisuddho antarāyike hi dhammehi. Paripuṇṇassa patta cīvaraṃ. Nāgo saṅghaṃ upasampadaṃ yācati. Āyasmatā tissena upajjhāyena. Saṅgho nāgaṃ upasampādeti. Āyasmatā tissena upajjhāyena. Yassāyasmato khamati nāgassa upasampadā. Āyasmatā tissena upajjhāyena. So tuṇhassa. Yassa nakkhamati so bhāsey‍ya.

Upasampanno saṅghena nāgo āyasmatā tissena upajjhāyena. Khamati saṅghassa. Tasmā tuṇhī, evametaṃ dhārayāmi.

Instruction given at the conclusion of the Higher Ordination

Tāvadeva jāyāmetabbā. Utuppamāṇaṃ ācikkhitabbaṃ divasabhāgo ācikkhitabbo. Saṅgīti ācikkhitabbā. Cattāro nissayā ācikkhitabbā. Cattāri akaraṇiyāni ācikkhitabbāni.

Since our Fortunate, Worthy, Perfectly Self-Enlightened Buddha, who has traversed all knowable realms and is greatly compassionate, has thus declared, and since the Great Community of monks, endowed with virtues such as good conduct, has consecrated the Venerable One with the supremely pure Higher Ordination, it has been permitted to declare these at the time of receiving the Higher Ordination in this sima (ordination hall): the measure of the shadow, the measure of the season, the division of the day, all of these, the four supports, and the four prohibitions. Therefore, please listen attentively to our words.

Venerable One, who has been consecrated with the supremely pure Higher Ordination precept! You, on the ................ day, the ................... tithi of the ................ month, in the ................ year of the Buddhist Era, corresponding to the ................ day of the ................ month, in the ................ year of the Common Era, at ................ in the ................ sima (ordination hall), were raised from the state of a novice and brought to the state of a bhikkhu with Higher Ordination by the Great Community of monks, led by ................... Bearing this in mind for the duration of your life, protect the Higher Ordination precept you have now received as you would your own life, without breaking even the smallest training rule.

The Four Resources

Venerable one, who has entered the Dispensation of the Buddha desiring to realize Nibbana having seen the fear in the round of rebirths! Listen to the Four Resources prescribed by the Great Compassionate One for the maintenance of the life of a renunciant.

(1) “Piṇḍiyālopa bhojanaṃ nissāya pabbajjā. Tattha te yāvajīvaṃ ussāho karaṇīyo. Atireka lābho saṅghabhattaṃ. Uddesabhattaṃ, nimantaṇaṃ, salākabhattaṃ, pakkhikaṃ, uposathikaṃ, pāṭipadikaṃ.”

Friend! This going-forth is on account of food received by alms-gathering. Therein effort is to be made by you for the rest of your life. Extra allowances are: a meal for the entire Community of monks, a meal for a specific number of monks, an invitation, a meal given by tickets, a meal given on a day of the half-month, a meal given on an Uposatha day, and a meal given on the day after an Uposatha day; these should be accepted as extra allowances.

(2) “Paṃsukūla cīvaraṃ nissāya pabbajjā, tat‍tha te yāvajīvaṃ ussāho karaṇīyo. Atireka lābho khomaṃ kappāsikaṃ. Kosey‍yaṃ. Kambalaṃ. Sāṇaṃ. Bhaṅgaṃ.”

The going-forth is on account of rag-robes. Therein effort is to be made by you for the rest of your life. If, through the power of your previous merit, there should accrue to you extra allowances: robes made of linen, cotton, silk, wool, coarse hemp, or a mixture of these, they may be used as extra allowances.

(3) “Rukkhamūlasenāsanaṃ nissāya pabbajjā. Tat‍tha te yāvajīvaṃ ussāho karaṇīyo. Atireka lābho vihāro. Aḍḍhayogo. Pāsādo. Hammiyaṃ. Guhā”

The going-forth is on account of a lodging at the foot of a tree. Therein effort is to be made by you for the rest of your life, dwelling at the foot of a tree. If, through the power of your previous merit, there should accrue to you extra allowances: a monastery, a curved house, a long house, a mansion, or a cave, these also may be used.

(4) “Pūtimuttabhesajjaṃ nissāya pabbajjā. Tat‍tha te yāvajīvaṃ ussāho karaṇīyo. Atireka lābho sappi. Navanītaṃ telaṃ. Madhu. Phānitaṃ”

The going-forth is on account of fermented urine as medicine. Therein effort is to be made by you for the rest of your life to maintain yourself with fermented urine as medicine. If, through the power of your previous merit, there should accrue to you extra allowances: ghee, fresh butter, oil, honey, or molasses, these may be used as extra allowances.

The Four Unperformables

Now, listen to the Four Unperformables.

(1) “Upasampannena bhikkhunā methuno dhammo na patisevitabbo, antamaso tiracchāna gatāyapi. Yo bhikkhu methunaṃ dhammaṃ patisevati, assamaṇo hoti asakyaputtiyo. Seyyathāpi nāma puriso sīsacchinno abhabbo tena sarīra bandhanena jīvituṃ. Evameva bhikkhu methunaṃ dhammaṃ patisevitvā assamaṇo hoti asakyaputtiyo. Taṃ te yāvajīvaṃ akaraṇīyaṃ”

By a bhikkhu who has received Higher Ordination, sexual intercourse should not be engaged in, not even with an animal. If any bhikkhu engages in sexual intercourse, by that he is no recluse, no son of the Sakyan. Just as a man whose head has been cut off is incapable of living again by that body connection, even so, a bhikkhu who has engaged in sexual intercourse is no recluse, no son of the Sakyan. Therefore, by you, that sexual intercourse should not be done as long as life lasts.

(2) “Upasampannena bhikkhunā adinnaṃ theyya saṅkhātaṃ na ādātabbaṃ antamaso tiṇasalākaṃ upādāya. Yo bhikkhu pādaṃ vā pādārahaṃ vā atirekapādaṃ vā adinnaṃ theyyasaṃkhātaṃ ādiyati assamaṇo hoti asakyaputtiyo. Seyyathāpi nāma paṇḍupalāso bandhanā pamutto abhabbo haritatthāya. Evameva bhikkhu pādaṃ vā pādārahaṃ vā atirekapādaṃ vā adinnaṃ theyyasaṃkhātaṃ ādiyitvā assamaṇo hoti asakyaputtiyo. Taṃ te yāvajīvaṃ akaraṇīyaṃ.”

By a bhikkhu who has received Higher Ordination, from a blade of grass upwards, that which is not given by the owners should not be taken with a thievish intent. If any bhikkhu takes with a thievish intent a *pada* of wealth, or an object worth a *pada*, or an object worth more than a *pada*, he is no recluse, no son of the Sakyan. Just as a withered leaf detached from its stalk is incapable of becoming green again, even so, a bhikkhu who has taken with a thievish intent a *pada* of wealth, or an object worth a *pada*, or an object worth more than a *pada*, is no recluse, no son of the Sakyan. That, by you, should not be done as long as life lasts.

(3) “Upasampannena bhikkhunā sañcicca pāṇo jīvitā na voropetabbo antamaso kuntha kipillikaṃ upādāya. Yo bhikkhu sañcicca manussaviggahaṃ jīvitā voropeti. Antamaso gabbha pātanaṃ upādāya. Assamaṇo hoti asakyaputtiyo. Seyyathāpi nāma puthusīlā dvidhā bhinnā appaṭisandhikā hoti. Evameva bhikkhu sañcicca manussa viggahaṃ jīvitā voropetvā assamaṇo hoti asakyaputtiyo. Taṃ te yāvajīvaṃ akaraṇīyaṃ”

By a bhikkhu who has received Higher Ordination, from an ant upwards, a living being should not be intentionally deprived of life. If any bhikkhu intentionally deprives a human being of life, even by causing an abortion, that bhikkhu is no recluse, no son of the Sakyan. Just as a slab of rock split in two is incapable of being joined again, even so, a bhikkhu who has intentionally deprived a human being of life is no recluse, no son of the Sakyan. That, by you, should not be done as long as life lasts.

(4) “Upasampannena bhikkhunā uttarimanussa dhammo na ullapitabbo antamaso suññāgāre abhiramāmīti. Yo bhikkhu pāpiccho icchāpakato asantaṃ abhūtaṃ uttarimanussadhammaṃ ullapati jhānaṃ vā vimokkhaṃ vā samādhiṃ vā samāpattiṃ vā maggaṃ vā phalaṃ vā. Assamaṇo hoti. Asakya puttiyo. Seyyathāpi nāma tālo matthakacchinno abhabbo puna virūḷhiyā. Evameva bhikkhu pāpiccho icchāpakato asantaṃ abhūtaṃ uttarīmanussadhammaṃ ullapitvā assamaṇo hoti. Asakyaputtiyo. Taṃ te yāvajīvaṃ akaraṇīyaṃ”

By a bhikkhu who has received Higher Ordination, even by saying, “I dwell delighting in an empty dwelling,” a superhuman state should not be declared. If any bhikkhu, being of evil wishes, overcome by evil wishes, declares a non-existent, unreal superhuman state in himself, such as jhāna, or vimokkha, or samādhi, or samāpatti, or magga, or phala, by that he is no recluse, no son of the Sakyan. Just as a palm tree whose crown has been cut off is incapable of growing again, even so, a bhikkhu, being of evil wishes, overcome by evil wishes, having declared a non-existent superhuman state in himself, is no recluse, no son of the Sakyan. That, by you, should not be done as long as life lasts.

Higher Ordination of Two or Three Persons at Once

At that time, disputes arose among the Candidates for Higher Ordination regarding who should receive Higher Ordination first. When this was reported to the Buddha, He permitted the Higher Ordination of two or three persons with the same Preceptor by a single announcement, saying: “Anujānāmi bhikkhave dve tayo ekānusāvane kātuṃ. Tañca kho ekena upajjhāyena, natveva nānupajjhāyena”. However, the formal procedure for this is not found in the Vinaya Pali. In the formal procedure books of Burma, it is presented thus:

“Suṇātu me bhante saṅgho. Ayaṃ ca nāgo ayaṃ ca datto āyasmato tissassa upasampadāpekkhā, parisuddhā antarāyikehi dhammehi. Paripuṇṇimesaṃ pattacīvaraṃ. Nāgo ca datto ca saṅghaṃ upasampadaṃ yācanti. Āyasmatā tissena upajjhāyena. Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ. Saṅgho nāgaṃ ca dattaṃ ca upasampādey‍ya āyasmatā tissena upajjhāyena. Esā ñatti.
Let the Sangha hear me, venerable sirs. This Candidate and this Datta are Candidates for Higher Ordination of the Venerable Tissa; they are pure of obstructing conditions. Their bowl and robes are complete. The Candidate and Datta request Higher Ordination from the Community, with the Venerable Tissa as Preceptor. If the Sangha is ready, the Community should confer Higher Ordination upon the Candidate and Datta, with the Venerable Tissa as Preceptor. This is the Motion.

Suṇātu me bhanto saṅgho. Ayaṃ ca nāgo ayaṃ ca datto āyasmato tissassa upasampadā pekkhā. Parisuddhā antarāyikehi dhammehi. Paripuṇṇimesaṃ pattacīvaraṃ. Nāgo ca datto ca saṅghaṃ upasampadaṃ yācanti. Āyasmatā tissena upajjhāyena. Saṅgho nāgaṃ ca dattaṃ ca upasampādeti. Āyasmatā tissena upajjhāyena. Yassāyasmato khamati nāgassa ca dattassa ca upasampadā. Āyasmatā. Tissena upajjhāyena. So tuṇhassa. Yassa nakkhamati. So bhāsey‍ya.
Let the Sangha hear me, venerable sirs. This Candidate and this Datta are Candidates for Higher Ordination of the Venerable Tissa. They are pure of obstructing conditions. Their bowl and robes are complete. The Candidate and Datta request Higher Ordination from the Community, with the Venerable Tissa as Preceptor. The Community confers Higher Ordination upon the Candidate and Datta, with the Venerable Tissa as Preceptor. Whosoever of the venerable ones approves of the Higher Ordination of the Candidate and Datta, with the Venerable Tissa as Preceptor, should keep silent. Whosoever does not approve, should speak.

Dutiyampi etamat‍thaṃ vadāmi. -pe-
I state this matter a second time. -etc.-

Tatiyampi etamat‍thaṃ vadāmi. -pe- so bhāsey‍ya.
I state this matter a third time. -etc.- Whosoever does not approve, should speak.

Upasampannā saṅghena nāgo ca datto ca āyasmatā tissena upajjhāyena. Khamati saṅghassa. Tasmā tuṇhī. Evametaṃ dhārayāmi.”
The Candidate and Datta have received Higher Ordination from the Community, with the Venerable Tissa as Preceptor. The Community approves. Therefore, it is Silent. Thus I bear this in mind.

Persons Ineligible for Higher Ordination

The thirteen persons—namely, the eleven types of persons such as the paṇḍaka mentioned above, the person who has committed a pārājika offense, and the person who has not completed twenty years of age—are ineligible for Higher Ordination. Even if the Act of Higher Ordination is performed, the Higher Ordination does not take effect for them. If those with other disqualifications are given Higher Ordination, the officiating Community of monks incurs an offense of wrong-doing (dukkaṭa). However, the Higher Ordination takes effect for them. All those who are designated as unfit for Going Forth (pabbajjā) are also unfit for Higher Ordination. Nevertheless, if those such as those with severed hands (hatthacchinna) and others are given Higher Ordination, the Higher Ordination takes effect for them. By that act, the officiating Community of monks and the teachers and preceptors incur an offense. Therefore, in the Monastic Procedures Commentary named Samantapāsādikā, it is stated: “Yesaṃ ca pabbajjā paṭikkhittā upasampadāpi tesaṃ paṭikkhittā va. Sace pana saṅgho upasampādeti sabbepi hatthacchinnādayo suvupasampannā, kārakasaṅgho pana ācariyupajjhāyā ca āpattito na muccati”.

The Age of the Candidate for Higher Ordination

The age of the candidate for Higher Ordination is a matter of particular importance. “Yo pana bhikkhu jānaṃ ūnavīsati vassaṃ puggalaṃ upasampādey‍ya so ca puggalo anupasampanno. Teca bhikkhu gārayhā idaṃ tasmiṃ pācittiyaṃ” — Thus, the Tathāgata has laid down an offense of Expiation (Pācittiya) for a Preceptor who confers Higher Ordination upon a person known to be under twenty years of age. For the offense of conferring Higher Ordination on other disqualified persons, only an offense of Wrongdoing (Dukkaṭa) is prescribed, not an offense of Expiation. Therefore, we state that the age of the candidate for Higher Ordination must be considered with special care. The age of the candidate for Higher Ordination should be calculated not from birth, but from the moment of conception. This is to be understood from the following:

“Yaṃ bhikkhave! mātukucchismiṃ paṭhamaṃ cittaṃ uppannaṃ. Paṭhamaṃ viññāṇaṃ pātubhūtaṃ. Tadupādāya sāvassa jāti. Anujānāmi bhikkhave gabbhavīsaṃ upasampādetuṃ”

This is to be known from the teaching of the Monastic Procedures. A person who spent ten months in the mother's womb may be given Higher Ordination when nineteen years and two months have elapsed since birth. A person who spent nine months in the mother's womb may be given Higher Ordination after nineteen years and three months have elapsed since birth. There is no offense if one confers Higher Ordination by mistake, thinking a person who has not completed twenty years has indeed completed them. However, the Higher Ordination is not valid for the candidate whose age was misunderstood. If he, after ten years of seniority, becomes a Preceptor and confers Higher Ordination on a pupil, the Higher Ordination is valid for the pupil provided there were enough other bhikkhus present to complete the quorum for the formal act, excluding him. For that person who lives as a monk without knowing that he was ordained under twenty years of age, such living is not an obstacle to heaven or liberation. Even if he has reached old age, if he comes to know for certain that he was ordained under-age, he must receive Higher Ordination again. Since presenting oneself as one who has received Higher Ordination after knowing the truth is a deception, it becomes an obstacle for him.

“Anāpatti ūnavīsativassaṃ paripuṇṇavīsati saññīti ettha kiñcāpi upasampādentassa anāpatti puggalo pana anupasampannova hoti. Sace pana so dasavassaccayena aññaṃ upasampādeti. Taṃ ce muñcitvā gaṇo pūrati. Supasampanno so pi ca yāva na jānāti. Tāvassa neva saggantarāyo na mokkhantarāyo. Ñatvā pana puna upasampajjitabbaṃ” — Thus states the Vinaya Commentary.

Monks Suitable to Be an Upajjhāya

Not all are suitable to become an Upajjhāya and give novice ordination and Higher Ordination to disciples. A bhikkhu who, without being suitable, becomes an Upajjhāya and gives novice ordination and Higher Ordination to disciples incurs an offence of dukkata.

“Na bhikkhave! Bālena abyattena upasampādetabbo. Anujānāmi bhikkhave! Byattena bhikkhunā paṭibalena dasavassena vā atireka dasavassena vā upasampādetuṃ” This is the word of the Buddha.

“Bhikkhus, Higher Ordination should not be given by a foolish, inexperienced bhikkhu. Bhikkhus, I allow Higher Ordination to be given by a bhikkhu who is experienced, capable, and has ten years or more than ten years [since his Higher Ordination],” this is its meaning.

Monks suitable to be an Upajjhāya are called ‘parisūpatthāyaka bahussuta’. The qualities of a parisūpatthāyaka bahussuta are as follows:

Being proficient in both the Bhikkhu and Bhikkhunī Vibhaṅgas of the Vinaya; knowing the discernment of what is a valid act and what is not; knowing the Vassakkhandhaka; having mastered either the Mūlapaṇṇāsaka from the Majjhima Nikāya, or the Mahāvagga from the Dīgha Nikāya, or the three lower sections or the Mahāvagga from the Saṃyutta Nikāya, or half of the Aṅguttara Nikāya, or the Dhammapada with its commentary or the Jātaka book from the Khuddaka Nikāya; and having ten years since Higher Ordination—these are the qualities of a parisūpatthāyaka bahussuta.

After Higher Ordination:

A newly ordained bhikkhu must reside with his Upajjhāya, associating with him, in the same place where the Upajjhāya resides, until five years have passed. A novice bhikkhu who resides separated from his Upajjhāya incurs an offence of dukkata with each dawn. An Upajjhāya who, having given Higher Ordination to others, then abandons them, also incurs an offence of dukkata due to breaking the observance. Unless one is a ‘nissaya muttaka bahussuta’, a bhikkhu must reside with his Upajjhāya or an ācariya for life.

Having memorized both Prātimokṣas; being proficient in four recitations (bāṇavara) from the Sutta Piṭaka; having practiced some method of discourse to teach those who come to him; having practiced three discourses for the purpose of anumodanā (rejoicing) on auspicious and inauspicious occasions; knowing about Monastic Procedures such as Uposatha and Pavāraṇā; having practiced some path of meditation; and having completed five years since Higher Ordination—these are the qualities of a nissaya muttaka bahussuta.

Taking Dependence

A bhikkhu who has not been released from dependence due to some reason such as the passing away of his preceptor, if the preceptor's dependence is lost, that bhikkhu should live by taking dependence from a venerable, learned elder who is diligent in attendance. The bhikkhu taking dependence should approach the dependence-preceptor, arrange his upper robe over one shoulder, squat, and having paid homage, should take dependence by repeating the phrase “Ācāriyo me bhante hohi. Āyasmato nissāya vacchāmi” three times. The dependence-preceptor should accept it by saying “Pāsādikena sampādetha”. The words Sāhu, Lahu, Opāyikaṃ, Patirūpaṃ are also suitable for this purpose.

If a bhikkhu who has observed many rains retreats but lacks sufficient learning to be released from dependence cannot find an elder from whom to take dependence, he should take dependence from a younger, venerable, learned bhikkhu who is diligent in attendance. He should pay homage even to a teacher who is younger than himself. However, the word “Bhante” should not be used when addressing a junior. When an elder bhikkhu takes dependence from a junior bhikkhu, he should, in the presence of the dependence-preceptor, arrange his upper robe over one shoulder, squat, and having paid homage, say “Ācāriyo me āvuso hohi. Āyasmato nissāya vacchāmi” three times. When asking permission to enter a village, he should also squat, pay homage, and say “Gāmappavesanaṃ āpucchāmi ācariya”. The same procedure should be followed when asking questions.

Between the preceptor and the dependence-preceptor, the preceptor is primary. Dependence should be taken when one is separated from the preceptor. If, while the preceptor resides in another locality and one is living having taken dependence from another elder, the preceptor returns there, the dependence taken by the pupil ceases the moment he sees his preceptor. (It becomes null and void.) If the preceptor departs again, since the previously taken dependence has ceased, dependence must be taken again. When one receives Higher Ordination, the preceptor one has taken remains valid at all times. There is no need to take another. Dependence, however, must be taken again and again whenever it ceases.

Adhiṭṭhāna and Vikappana Procedures

Formal Resolve of the Bowl

Since it has been declared: “Dasāhaparamaṃ atireka patto dhāretabbo. Taṃ atikkāmayato nissaggiyaṃ pācittiyaṃ” (An extra bowl may be kept for a maximum of ten days. For one who exceeds that, there is an offence of Nissaggiya Pacittiya), a bhikkhu who keeps an extra bowl for more than ten days incurs a Nissaggiya Pacittiya offence. Therefore, a bowl must either be formally resolved or formally assigned. An extra bowl is one that has not been so treated. A bowl that should be formally resolved as a bowl is one that is neither too small nor too large, but is of a suitable size for use and has been properly finished and colored. A bowl that does not meet these criteria incurs no offence if used as a mere utensil.

A bowl received at Higher Ordination must be formally resolved within ten days. If, while one bowl is formally resolved, a new bowl needs to be formally resolved, since a bhikkhu cannot keep two bowls, the old bowl must either be given to another or formally withdrawn. When formally withdrawing a bowl, if the bowl is nearby, take it in hand or touch it and say “Imaṃ pattaṃ paccuddharāmi” (I formally withdraw this bowl) three times. If the bowl is far away, considering its location, say “Etaṃ pattaṃ paccuddharāmi” (I formally withdraw that bowl) three times. When formally resolving a bowl, if the bowl is nearby, say “Imaṃ pattaṃ adhiṭṭhāmi” (I formally resolve this bowl) three times, and if it is far away, considering its location, say “Etaṃ pattaṃ adhiṭṭhāmi” (I formally resolve that bowl) three times.

Breaking the Formal Resolve

“Accheda dāna gāhehi-vibbhamā maraṇuḍḍhaṭā

Liṅga sikkhāhi chiddesu-pattodhiṭṭhāna mujjhati.”

As indicated by the above, the formal resolve of a bowl ceases due to nine reasons: seizure by thieves, giving to others, taking by trusted persons, abandoning the robes, death, formal withdrawal, change of gender, rejection of Higher Ordination, and becoming so holed that rice grains fall out. If the formal resolve is broken due to a hole, it must be repaired and formally resolved again. If not done so, a Nissaggiya Pacittiya offence is incurred after ten days.

Formal Assignment of a Bowl

If a bhikkhu who has formally resolved one bowl receives other bowls and wishes to keep them in his possession, he must formally assign them. If he keeps them without doing so, a Nissaggiya Pacittiya offence is incurred after ten days. Formal assignment should be done as follows: Take the bowl to a competent bhikkhu, squat, and say the phrase “Imaṃ pattaṃ tuyhaṃ vikappemi” (I formally assign this bowl to you) three times, and give it to that bhikkhu. Formal assignment is not a gift or an offering, but a Monastic Procedure. Therefore, that bhikkhu must return it, saying the phrase “Mayhaṃ santakaṃ paribhuñja vā vissajjehi vā yathāpaccayaṃ karohi” (Use what belongs to me, or dispose of it, or do as you deem fit) three times. This act is called formal return (paccuddhāra).

After the formal return, keeping and using that bowl for as long as desired incurs no offence. When formally assigning multiple bowls, one should say “Ime patte tuyhaṃ vikappemi” (I formally assign these bowls to you), and during the formal return, “Mayhaṃ santake paribhūñja vā vissajjehi vā yathā paccayaṃ karohi” (Use what belongs to me, or dispose of them, or do as you deem fit). If the bowl is not nearby for formal assignment, tell the other bhikkhu where your bowl is, and considering its location, say “Etaṃ pattaṃ tuyhaṃ vikappemi” (I formally assign that bowl to you), or if there are many bowls, “Ete patte tuyhaṃ vikappemi” (I formally assign those bowls to you).

Size of the Bowl

“Tayo pattassa vaṇṇā ukkaṭṭho patto majjhimo patto omako patto. Ukkaṭṭho nāma patto aḍḍhāḷhako danaṃ gaṇhāti. Catubhāgaṃ khādanaṃ tadupiyaṃ ca vyañjanaṃ. Majjhimo nāma patto nālikodanaṃ gaṇhāti. Catubhāgaṃ khādanaṃ tadupiyaṃ ca vyañjanaṃ. Omako nāma patto patthodanaṃ gaṇhāti.
