The meaning of this is: ‘Since it is difficult to use robes determined as the three robes at all times, even a bhikkhu who uses robes determined as accessory cloths, having revoked those robes determined as accessory cloths, and having determined them according to the method of the three robes at a time close to the spreading of the Kathina, is indeed suitable to spread the Kathina.’

Therefore, if a bhikkhu who uses robes determined as accessory cloths (parikkhāracola adhiṭṭhāna) has to receive a Kathina robe, that bhikkhu should take a double-layered robe, a single-layered robe, and an undergarment from the robes he uses as accessory cloths, and say: “Imāni cīvarāni parikkhāracolāni paccuddhārāmi” (I revoke these robes determined as accessory cloths). Having revoked them, he should then determine them one by one according to the method of the three robes (ticīvarādhiṭṭhāna vidhi). Then, he should revoke one of those three robes and, in its place, determine the Kathina robe and spread the Kathina. Only by doing so are the acts of revocation and determination (paccuddhāra-adhiṭṭhāna kamma) complete.

The Eight Topics

Although the spreading of the Kathina is performed by one bhikkhu, it also pertains to the bhikkhus who rejoice in it. Therefore, in the Parivāra Pāli, it was declared: “Dvinnaṃ puggalānaṃ atthataṃ hoti kaṭhinaṃ, atthārakassa ca anumodakassa ca”, meaning ‘The Kathina is spread for two persons: the spreader and the rejoicer.’

There are eight conditions by which that Kathina-spreading procedure, which pertains to both the spreader and the rejoicer, ceases, loses its validity, or becomes annulled. The annulment of the Kathina-spreading is called ‘Kaṭhinuddhāra’ or ‘Kaṭhinubbhāra’. Upon the occurrence of Kaṭhinuddhāra, the five benefits (pañcānisamsa) obtained from the Kathina-spreading are also lost.

“Aṭṭhimā bhikkhave mātikā kaṭhinassa ubbhārāya, pakkamanantikā, niṭṭhānantikā, sanniṭṭhānantikā, nāsanantikā, savanantikā, āsāvacchedikā, sīmātikkantikā, sahubbhārāti”

Thus, in the Kathinakkhandhaka of the Mahāvagga Pāli, eight topics concerning the annulment of the Kathina (Kaṭhinuddhāra) have been declared. What is indicated by ‘mātikaṃ jānāti’ among the qualifications of a person suitable for Kathina-spreading is the knowledge of these eight topics. The topics should be known as follows:

1. Pakkamanantikā (Ending with Departure).

If a bhikkhu, having completed his robe, takes it and departs from the place where he observed the rains-residence with no intention of returning, then the annulment of the Kathina (Kaṭhinuddhāra) occurs for that bhikkhu as soon as he crosses the boundary of the precincts. Thereafter, that bhikkhu loses the five benefits (pañcānisamsa) obtained from the spreading of the Kathina. This annulment of the Kathina is called ‘Pakkamanantika’ because it ends with departing from the monastery.

2. Niṭṭhānantikā (Ending with Completion).

If a bhikkhu, having received cloth at the place where he observed the rains-residence, takes it and goes to another monastery, and outside the boundary of the rains-residence, he resolves, “I will not return to the monastery where I observed the rains-residence,” then the validity of the Kathina-spreading (Kaṭhinatthāra) for that bhikkhu lasts only until he completes making a robe from the cloth he took. As soon as the work on the robe is completed, the annulment of the Kathina (Kaṭhinuddhāra) occurs for that bhikkhu. This annulment of the Kathina is called ‘Niṭṭhānantika’ because it ends with the completion of the robe.

3. Sanniṭṭhānantikā (Ending with Resolution).

If a bhikkhu, having taken the cloth he received from the place where he observed the rains-residence and departed from the monastery, resolves, “I will not return to the place where I observed the rains-residence, nor will I make this robe,” then the annulment of the Kathina (Kaṭhinuddhāra) occurs for that bhikkhu. This annulment of the Kathina is called ‘Sanniṭṭhānantika’ because it ends with the resolution.

4. Nāsanantikā (Ending with Loss).

If a bhikkhu, having received robe-cloth at the place where he observed the rains-residence and departed, resolves there, “I will make this robe right here, and I will not return to the place where I observed the rains-residence,” and while he is making that robe, the robe is lost by theft or in any other way, then the annulment of the Kathina (Kaṭhinuddhāra) occurs for that bhikkhu. This annulment of the Kathina is called ‘Nāsanantikā’ because it ends with the loss.

5. Savanantikā (Ending with Hearing).

A bhikkhu for whom the Kathina has been spread takes the cloth he received and departs with the intention of returning to the monastery later. There, the bhikkhu thinks, ‘I will make this robe right here.’ The bhikkhu makes the robe there. While that bhikkhu is staying, having completed the work on the robe, the news reaches him that ‘the Kathina has been annulled by the Community of monks in the monastery.’ Thereupon, the annulment of the Kathina (Kaṭhinuddhāra) also occurs for that bhikkhu. This annulment of the Kathina is called ‘Savanantikā’ because it ends with hearing the news.

6. Āsāvacchedikā (Ending with the Cutting Off of Hope).

A bhikkhu for whom the Kathina has been spread, not having received robes at the place where he observed the rains-residence, departs with the hope of receiving robes. Outside the monastery where he observed the rains-residence, that bhikkhu thinks, “I will not return to the place where I observed the rains-residence; I will stay here and hope for robes.” That bhikkhu stays there, reaching the place where he hopes for robes. If robes are not received from the place of hope, and that bhikkhu’s hope is cut off, then the annulment of the Kathina (Kaṭhinuddhāra) occurs for that bhikkhu. This annulment of the Kathina is called ‘Āsāvacchedikā’ because it occurs by the cutting off of hope.

7. Sīmātikkantikā (Ending with Transgression of the Boundary).

A bhikkhu for whom the Kathina has been spread takes the robe-cloth and departs with the intention of returning to the monastery. He makes the robe there. With the intention that ‘after the work on the robe is finished, that bhikkhu will return to the monastery,’ he stays there until the end of the Kathina annulment period. The annulment of the Kathina (Kaṭhinuddhāra) occurs for that bhikkhu by the expiration of the time. This annulment of the Kathina is called ‘Sīmātikkantikā’ because it occurs by transgressing the boundary, which is the last day of the robe period.

8. Sahubbhārā (Simultaneous Annulment).

A bhikkhu for whom the Kathina has been spread takes the robe and departs with the intention of returning to that monastery. He completes the robe and spends time there with the hope of returning, and then returns to the monastery at the time when the bhikkhus in the monastery annul the Kathina. The annulment of the Kathina (Kaṭhinuddhāra) occurs for that bhikkhu simultaneously with the annulment for the bhikkhus in the monastery. This annulment of the Kathina is called ‘Sahubbhārā’ because it occurs simultaneously with the annulment for the bhikkhus in the monastery. Details regarding the annulments of the Kathina should be known from the Kaṭhinakkhandhaka.

Two Obstacles

There are two obstacles to be known concerning the kaṭhina: the obstacle of dwelling (āvāsa-paḷibodha) and the obstacle of robes (cīvara-paḷibodha). The obstacle of dwelling is the necessity to reside in the monastery where the kaṭhina was spread, and if one departs from it, to maintain the expectation of returning. The obstacle of robes is that the bhikkhu's robe is not yet made or not yet finished, or that there is an expectation of obtaining a robe.

“Katame ca bhikkhave! dve kaṭhinassa paḷibodhā? Āvāsapaḷibodho ca cīvarapaḷibodho ca kathañca bhikkhave! āvāsapaḷibodho hoti? Idha bhikkhave! bhikkhu vasati vā yasmiṃ āvāse sāpekkhā vā pakkamati paccessati evaṃ kho bhikkhave! āvāsapaḷibodho hoti. Kathaṃ ca bhikkhave! cīvarapaḷibodho hoti? Idha bhikkhave! bhikkhuno cīvaraṃ akataṃ vā hoti vippakataṃ vā cīvarāsā vā anupacchinnā evaṃ kho bhikkhave! cīvarapaḷibodho hoti. Ime kho bhikkhave! kaṭhinassa dve paḷibodhā.”

Thus, the two obstacles are declared in the Kaṭhinakkhandhaka. The obstacle of dwelling (āvāsa-paḷibodha) persists as long as there is an expectation of residing in the monastery where the kaṭhina was spread. The obstacle of dwelling ceases when the expectation regarding the monastery is abandoned. The obstacle of robes (cīvara-paḷibodha) persists as long as there is a desire to make or obtain a robe. The obstacle of robes ceases when the robe is made, or when the cloth is destroyed, or when the expected robe is not received and the expectation is broken. The aforementioned eight types of kaṭhinoddhāra (removal of the kaṭhina) occur when these two obstacles are cut off. For a bhikkhu who has cut off the obstacle of dwelling and departed from the monastery, the validity of the kaṭhina-spreading remains until the obstacle of robes is cut off. After the obstacle of robes is cut off, that validity ends. Thereafter, that bhikkhu does not have the five advantages (pañcānisaṃsa). For a bhikkhu whose obstacle of robes has been cut off, the kaṭhinoddhāra occurs simultaneously with the abandonment of the expectation regarding the monastery.

Monastic Procedure for Removing the Kathina

If the Kathina has been spread in a certain monastery, all robes arising in that monastery until the end of the winter season belong to the bhikkhus who spread the Kathina. They do not belong to others. Therefore, if some faithful person wishes to offer many robes to the Saṅgha so that they may also belong to other bhikkhus, and requests the Saṅgha to perform the removal of the Kathina, the Saṅgha must perform the removal of the Kathina. Thereafter, all robes received by that monastery must be distributed equally among all bhikkhus.

The Formal Announcement for the Revocation of the Kathina

The formal announcement for the revocation of the Kathina is as follows:

“Suṇātu me bhante! Saṅgho. Yadi Saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, saṅgho kaṭhinaṃ uddhareyya. Esā ñatti.

Suṇātu me bhante! Saṅgho. Saṅgho kaṭhinaṃ uddharati. Yassāyasmato khamati kaṭhinassa ubbhāro. So tuṇhasssa. Yassa nakkhamati so bhāseyya. Ubbhataṃ Saṅghena kaṭhinaṃ. Khamati Saṅghassa. Tasmā tuṇhī. Evametaṃ dhārayāmi.”

If the benefits based on the revocation of the Kathina are greater than the benefits based on the spreading of the Kathina, then the Kathina should be revoked. If the benefits based on the revocation are few, it should not be revoked. For the sake of preserving the donor's faith, the Kathina should also be revoked when the benefits are equal. Opposing a righteous revocation of the Kathina incurs an offense.

The Benefits of Kathina

To bhikkhus who have spread the Kathina, there are five benefits received according to the Buddha's injunction due to that Monastic Procedure. This is called ‘Kaṭhināniṃsa’ (Benefits of Kathina). Furthermore, the robes and other requisites received due to the Kathina robe are also called Kaṭhināniṃsa. The reward received by the Kathina donor from that merit is also called by that name. It should be known that the five benefits received by the bhikkhus who have spread the Kathina, according to the Buddha's injunction, are not a result of that merit. The fivefold benefits received by the bhikkhu are declared thus in the Kaṭhinakkhandhaka:

Atthatakaṭhinānaṃ vo bhikkhave! Pañcakappissanti anāmantavāro, asamādānavāro, gaṇabhojanaṃ, yāvadatthacīvaraṃ, yo ca tattha cīvaruppādo so nesaṃ bhavissati.

Hereby it is declared that five matters become allowable for bhikkhus who have spread the Kathina due to that reason. These are as follows:-

1. Anāmantacāro (Going without prior invitation).

“Yo pana bhikkhu nimantito sa bhatto samāno santaṃ bhikkhuṃ anāpucchā purebhattaṃ vā pacchābhattaṃ vā kulesu cārittaṃ āpajjeyya aññatra samayā pācittiyaṃ tatrāyaṃ samayo cīvaradāna samayo, cīvarakārasamayo, ayaṃ tattha samayo.”

This is the sixth training rule in the Acelakavagga of the Pātimokkha. According to this training rule, the offense incurred by a bhikkhu residing in a monastery who goes to houses without asking another bhikkhu is not applicable to bhikkhus who have spread the Kathina. After the Kathina has been spread, freedom is obtained to frequent families without asking.

2. Asamādānacāro (Going without the three robes).

“Niṭṭhitacīvarasmiṃ pana bhikkhunā ubbhatasmiṃ kaṭhine. Ekarattaṃ’pi ce bhikkhu ticīvarena vippavaseyya aññatra bhikkhu sammutiyā, nissaggiyaṃ pācittiyaṃ.”

This is the second training rule in the Nissaggiya Pācittiyas of the Pātimokkha. According to this training rule, the offense incurred by a bhikkhu who spends a night separated from his determined set of three robes is not applicable to bhikkhus who have spread the Kathina.

3. Gaṇabhojanaṃ (Group meal).

“Gaṇabhojane aññatra samayā pācittiyaṃ. Tatthāyaṃ samayo. Gilānasamayo cīvaradānasamayo cīvarakārasamayo addhānagamanasamayo nāvābhirūhanasamayo mahāsamayo samaṇabhattasamayo ayaṃ tattha samayo.”

This is the second training rule in the Bhojanavagga of the Pātimokkha. According to this training rule, the offense incurred by partaking in a group meal is not applicable to bhikkhus who have spread the Kathina.

4. Yāvadattha Cīvaraṃ (As many robes as one wishes).

“Niṭṭhitacīvarasmiṃ pana bhikkhunā ubbhatasmiṃ kaṭhine dasāhaparamaṃ atirekacīvaraṃ dhāretabbaṃ: taṃ atikkāmayato nissaggiyaṃ pācittiyaṃ.”

This is the first training rule in the Nissaggiya Pācittiyas. According to this training rule, the offense incurred by keeping extra robes for more than ten days is not applicable to bhikkhus who have spread the Kathina.

5. Yo ca tattha Cīvaruppādo so nesaṃ bhavissati (Whatever robe-material arises there, that will be theirs).

If, within the boundary where the Kathina has been spread, robes belonging to the Community arise as rag-robes, or are offered by devotees as belonging to the Community, or from things produced on that land, or in any other way, during the period of Kathina benefits, all of those belong to the bhikkhus who have spread the Kathina. Apart from robes received communally, other requisites do not become theirs by the power of the Kathina spreading. Those belong to everyone. The reception of these five benefits is the special advantage for bhikkhus from the spreading of the Kathina.

The Period of Kathina Spreading and the Period of Benefits

“Kaṭhinassa atthāramaso jānitabboti. Vassānassa pacchimo māso jānitabboti.” This being a Pali passage from the Parivara, the last month of the rainy season should be known as the period for spreading the Kathina. That is: the twenty-nine days from the new moon of Vap to the full moon of Il. The efficacy of the Monastic Procedure of spreading the Kathina lasts from the day the Kathina is spread until the full moon Poya day of Medin, which is the last day of the winter season. Until then, the five benefits declared for bhikkhus who have spread the Kathina are present.

If there are extra robes that have not been determined or relinquished, their determination or relinquishment should be performed on that day. For all bhikkhus, whether they have spread the Kathina or not, the twenty-nine days of the last month of the rainy season constitute the robe period (cīvara-kāla). For bhikkhus who have spread the Kathina, the entire five months from the new moon of Vap until the full moon day of Medin constitute the robe period. During the robe period, keeping extra robes does not incur an offense.

Monks who Receive and Do Not Receive the Kathina Spreading

“Kaṭhinatthāraṃ ke labhanti? Ke na labhanti? Gaṇanavasena tāva pacchimakoṭiyā pañcajanā labhanti. Uddhaṃ satasahassampi pañcannaṃ heṭṭhā na labhanti. Vutthavassavasena purimikāya vassaṃ upagantvā paṭhamapavāraṇāya pavāritā labhanti. Jinnavassā vā pacchimikāya upagatā vā na labhanti.”

According to what is stated in the Vinaya Commentary, for the performance of the Kathina spreading Monastic Procedure, there must be at least five bhikkhus. The Kathina is spread by one bhikkhu. This can only be done with a robe given by the Community of monks to a bhikkhu. Therefore, apart from the bhikkhu who receives it, four other bhikkhus must be present to give the robe. That is why there must be at least five bhikkhus for a Kathina to be spread. The Kathina can only be spread by a bhikkhu who observed the Rains Retreat previously and performed the Pavarana at the first Pavarana. Any bhikkhu may assist in assigning the Kathina cloth. Only bhikkhus who observed the Rains Retreat previously and performed the Pavarana receive the benefits of the Kathina. Others do not receive them. Bhikkhus who observed the Rains Retreat and performed the Pavarana in another monastery also do not receive the benefits of a Kathina spread in one monastery. If there is even one bhikkhu who observed the Rains Retreat previously and performed the Pavarana in a certain place, that bhikkhu can spread the Kathina there with the assistance of other bhikkhus.

Unconcealed Mānatta

A bhikkhu who has fallen into a grave offense must inform another bhikkhu of that fact on the very same day. If he falls into an offense during the night, he must inform another bhikkhu that same night before the rise of dawn on the following day. If he fails to inform on that day without any obstacle, there is an additional offense of wrongdoing. The informing bhikkhu should approach another bhikkhu, arrange his upper robe over one shoulder, squat down, raise his joined palms, and inform him saying: “Tuyhaṃ santike ekaṃ āpattiṃ āvikaromi” or “ācikkhāmi” or “ārocemi” or “mama ekaṃ āpattiṃ āpannabhāvaṃ jānāhi”. Or, as is currently used during the confession of offenses, he should inform saying: “Ahaṃ bhante sabbāpattiyo āvikaromi”.

For bhikkhus who rise before dawn, pay homage to the Triple Gem, and confess their offenses daily, there is no concealment of offenses. To be rehabilitated from unconcealed grave offenses, one must observe Mānatta for six days and then undergo the Act of Rehabilitation. Regardless of the number of offenses, one may observe Mānatta for all of them at once for the sake of purification. When doing so, assemble the Community within a consecrated boundary to grant the Mānatta, which must then be observed. It should be performed as follows:

The Request for Unconcealed Mānatta

Ahaṃ bhante! Sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajjiṃ. Appaṭicchannāyo. So’haṃ bhante saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ appaṭicchannānaṃ chārattaṃ mānattaṃ yācāmi.

Within the consecrated boundary, before the Community, arrange the upper robe over one shoulder, squat down, raise the joined palms, and request Mānatta by reciting the above phrase three times.

The Formal Act for Granting Mānatta

“Suṇātu me bhante saṅgho. Ayaṃ (Tisso) bhikkhu sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajji, appaṭicchannāyo. So saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ appaṭicchannānaṃ chārattaṃ mānattaṃ yācati. Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, saṅgho (Tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ appaṭicchannānaṃ chārattaṃ mānattaṃ dadeyya. Esā ñatti.

Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho. Ayaṃ (Tisso) bhikkhu sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajji, appaṭicchannāyo. So saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ appaṭicchannānaṃ chārattaṃ mānattaṃ yācati. Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, saṅgho (Tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ appaṭicchannānaṃ chārattaṃ mānattaṃ deti. Yassāyasmato khamati, (Tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ appaṭicchannānaṃ chārattaṃ mānattassa dānaṃ, so tuṇhassa. Yassa nakkhamati so bhāseyya.

Dutiyampi etamatthaṃ vadāmi. Suṇātu me bhante! -pe- so bhāseyya.

Tatiyampi etamatthaṃ vadāmi. Suṇātu me bhante! -pe- so bhāseyya.

Dinnaṃ saṅghena (Tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ appaṭicchannānaṃ chārattaṃ mānattaṃ. Khamati saṅghassa tasmā tuṇhī. Evametaṃ dhārayāmi.”

Recite the formal act inserting the appropriate name.

The bhikkhu who has received Mānatta from the Community must undertake the Mānatta right there before the Community. The phrase for undertaking Mānatta is as follows:

“Mānattaṃ samādiyāmi. Vattaṃ samādiyāmi.”

Arrange the upper robe over one shoulder, squat down, pay homage to the Community, and recite the above phrase three times to complete the undertaking of Mānatta.

After the undertaking, announce the Mānatta to the Community. The phrase for announcement is as follows:

Ahaṃ bhante! Sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajji’. Appaṭicchannāyo. So’haṃ bhante saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ appaṭicchannānaṃ chārattaṃ mānattaṃ yāciṃ. Tassa me saṅgho tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ appaṭicchannānaṃ chārattaṃ mānattaṃ adāsi. So’haṃ mānattaṃ carāmi. Vedayāmahaṃ bhante! Vedayatīti maṃ saṅgho dhāretu.

Announce the Mānatta to the Community three times in this manner. A bhikkhu who has thus received Mānatta from the Community within a consecrated boundary may relinquish it whenever he wishes. He may then undertake it again without receiving it from the Community a second time. While undertaking and observing Mānatta or Probation, he must announce it to every bhikkhu he meets. Failure to do so results in an interruption of nights and an offense of wrongdoing for breach of duty. Interruption of nights means the loss of that day. If a bhikkhu observing Mānatta sees another bhikkhu even at a distance and is unable to announce it to him, that day is void. Another day must be observed in place of that day. Since it is not easy to observe duties and Mānatta everywhere, relinquish the Mānatta until you begin to observe it in a suitable place. Having undertaken it and announced it to the Community within the consecrated boundary where the Mānatta was received—

“Mānattaṃ nikkhipāmi. Vattaṃ nikkhipāmi.”

Relinquish it by saying this three times. Thereafter, until he undertakes it again, that bhikkhu is a regular bhikkhu. The practices to be observed by a bhikkhu undergoing Mānatta must be performed during the period he remains under the undertaking of Mānatta.

Purification is not attained merely by observing Mānatta. After observing Mānatta for six days, the Act of Rehabilitation must be performed to attain purification. To perform an Act of Rehabilitation, a group of no fewer than twenty bhikkhus must be assembled within a consecrated boundary. Since this is not a task that can be done immediately, relinquish the Mānatta and wait after the days are completed. A bhikkhu who is currently under the undertaking of Mānatta is eligible for the Act of Rehabilitation. Therefore, when the Community has assembled and is preparing for the Act of Rehabilitation, he should again undertake the Mānatta within that consecrated boundary, announce it to the Community, and then request the Act of Rehabilitation from the Community using the following phrase:

The Request for Abbhāna

Ahaṃ bhante! Sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajjiṃ appaṭicchannāyo. So’haṃ bhante saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ appaṭicchannānaṃ chārattaṃ mānattaṃ yāciṃ. Tassa me saṅgho tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ appaṭicchannānaṃ chārattaṃ mānattaṃ adāsi. So’haṃ bhante ciṇṇamānatto saṅghaṃ abbhānaṃ yācāmi.

Say this three times.

The Formal Act for Abbhāna

“Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho, ayaṃ (Tisso) bhikkhu sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpappattiyo āpajji, appaṭicchannāyo. So saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ appaṭicchannānaṃ chārattaṃ mānattaṃ yāci. Saṅgho (Tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ appaṭicchannānaṃ chārattaṃ mānattaṃ adāsi. So ciṇṇamānatto saṅghaṃ abbhānaṃ yācati. Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, saṅgho (Tissaṃ) bhikkhuṃ abbheyya. Esā ñatti.

Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho, ayaṃ (Tisso) bhikkhu sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajjiṃ appaṭicchannāyo. So saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ appaṭicchannānaṃ chārattaṃ mānattaṃ yāci. Saṅgho (Tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ appaṭicchannānaṃ chārattaṃ mānattaṃ adāsi. So ciṇṇamānatto saṅghaṃ abbhānaṃ yācati. Saṅgho Tissaṃ bhikkhuṃ abbheti. Yassāyasmato khamati (Tissassa) bhikkhuno abbhānaṃ, so tuṇhassa. Yassa nakkhamati so bhāseyya.

Dutiyampi etamatthaṃ vadāmi. Suṇātu me -pe- bhāseyya.

Tatiyampi etamatthaṃ vadāmi. Suṇātu me -pe- bhāseyya.

Abbhito saṅghena (Tisso) bhikkhu. Khamati saṅghassa tasmā tuṇhī. Evametaṃ dhārayāmi”

Concealment of Offenses

One who has fallen into a grave offense must inform another bhikkhu of that fact on the very same day. If he lives concealing the offense for any number of days without informing, the bhikkhu must undergo Probation for that number of days before observing Mānatta. If he performs the Act of Rehabilitation after observing only Mānatta without undergoing Probation, there is no purification regarding the concealed offenses.

“Āpatti ca hoti āpattisaññī ca. Pakatatto ca hoti pakatattasaññī ca, antarāyiko ca hoti antarāyikasaññī ca, pahu ca hoti pahusaññī ca, chādetukāmo ca hoti jādeti ca”

Concealment of offenses is shown in ten ways. If a grave offense occurs and with the perception that it is a grave offense, the bhikkhu does not inform on that day, he has concealed the offense. If he falls into an offense but does not know it and thus does not inform, he has not concealed it. If he thinks a grave offense is a light offense and does not inform, it is not concealed. If he falls into a grave offense and, not knowing whether it is grave or light, does not inform with the intention of hiding it, it is concealed.

If he is a regular bhikkhu who has not been suspended by the Community through an Act of Suspension, and with the perception that he is a regular bhikkhu, he does not inform of the offense, it is concealed. If he thinks, "I have been suspended by the Community; I am not a regular bhikkhu," and does not inform, the offense is not concealed. If he is not a regular bhikkhu but thinks he is and does not inform, the offense is not concealed.

If there is not even one of the ten obstacles that prevent going to a bhikkhu to inform of the offense, and with the perception that there is no obstacle, he does not inform, the offense is concealed. The ten obstacles are: obstacle from kings, obstacle from thieves, obstacle from fire, obstacle from water, obstacle from humans, obstacle from non-humans, obstacle from beasts, obstacle from reptiles, obstacle to life, and obstacle to the holy life. If there are obstacles on the way from where he resides to where the bhikkhu to be informed resides, and he does not go to inform, the offense is not concealed.

If he has the strength to go to a bhikkhu to inform, and knowing that, he does not go to inform, the offense is hidden. If he does not inform because he lacks the strength to go, the offense is not concealed. Even if he has the strength to go, but does not inform with the perception that he cannot go due to a minor ailment, it is not concealed.

If he does not inform with the intention of hiding the offense, he has concealed the offense. If the intention to hide arose earlier but changed later and he informs before dawn, he has not concealed it. For one who falls into an offense in a place where there are no bhikkhus and waits for the arrival of a bhikkhu to inform, even if days pass, the offense is not concealed. For one who travels to a bhikkhu of the same class to inform, even if many days pass, the offense is not concealed. If one who is waiting for a bhikkhu to inform does not inform the first bhikkhu he meets out of shame because that bhikkhu is his teacher or preceptor, he has concealed the offense. If he does not inform hostile persons who would spread the word and insult him, he has not concealed the offense.

“Āpattinukkhippamanantarāya

Pahuttatāyo tathā saññitā ca.

Jādetukāmo atha jādanāti

Jannā dasaṅgeharuṇuggamamhi.”

The meaning is: by these ten factors—the occurrence of a grave offense, being a bhikkhu who is not suspended, the absence of obstacles, the strength to go to a bhikkhu, having those four perceptions, the desire to hide the offense, and the act of hiding—the offense is concealed at the rise of dawn on the following day.

Combined Probation (Agghasamodhāna parivāsa).

Among the thirteen (Saṅghādisesa) offenses, the first and second are easily committed. One may even fall into them without realization. Consequently, the elders of old would fulfill the duties and penance once every year or every few years and undergo rehabilitation (Abbhāna) to ensure their purity. For a worldling bhikkhu, it is no simple task to remain at all times free from offense. Thus, a bhikkhu may incur many offenses; this is especially likely for those who are not well-versed in the Discipline.

A bhikkhu may have many offenses that were not concealed (Appaṭicchanna). He may also have many concealed (Paṭicchanna) offenses of one day's duration. Similarly, he may have many concealed offenses for durations of two, three, four, five, six, seven, eight, nine, ten, eleven, twelve, thirteen, or fourteen days. For those bhikkhus who arise before dawn, pay homage to the Triple Gem, and confess their offenses daily, there can be no concealed offenses. For those bhikkhus who pay homage and confess daily in the evening, concealed offenses cannot exceed one day's duration.

For bhikkhus who perform the (Uposatha) without fail, concealed offenses cannot exceed fourteen days. For a bhikkhu who does none of these, it is not easy to measure the period for which his offenses have been concealed. A bhikkhu who has both concealed and unconcealed offenses must first undergo probation (Parivāsa) for the concealed offenses to be purified. Thereafter, combining both types of offenses, he must undergo the six-day penance (Mānatta) for all of them. A bhikkhu who has many offenses—some concealed for one day, some for two days, and so on—should combine the offenses concealed for shorter periods with those concealed for the longest period, and undergo probation for the duration of that longest period. By doing so, purity is attained from all of them. The probation undertaken by combining offenses concealed for shorter durations with those concealed for the longest duration is called ‘Combined Probation’ (Agghasamodhāna parivāsa).

The method of its fulfillment is as follows: Herein is shown the procedure for undergoing probation by combining other offenses with those concealed for ten days. Following this, you should understand how to undergo probation for other durations of concealment, whether greater or lesser.

Formula for Requesting Combined Probation.

Ahaṃ bhante! sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajjiṃ. Sambahulā āpattiyo ekāhapaṭicchannāyo. Sambahulā āpattiyo dvihapaṭicchannāyo. Sambahulā āpattiyo tihapaṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo catūhapaṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo pañcāha paṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo chāhapaṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo sattāhapaṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo aṭṭhāhapaṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo navāhapaṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo, so’haṃ bhante! saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ yā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo. Tāsaṃ agghena samodhānaparivāsaṃ yācāmi.

Within the boundary, arrange your upper robe over one shoulder, sit in the squatting posture, pay homage to the Community, and request probation by reciting this formula three times.

The Formal Act Statement for Granting Combined Probation.

“Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho, ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajji. Sambahulā āpattiyo ekāhapaṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo dvīhapaṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo tīhapaṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo catūhapaṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo pañcāha paṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo chāhapaṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo sattāha paṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo aṭṭhāhapaṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo navāha paṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo, so saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ yā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo. Tāsaṃ agghena samodhānaparivāsaṃ yācati. Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, saṅgho (tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ yā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo. Tāsaṃ agghena samodhānaparivāsaṃ dadey‍ya. Esā ñatti.

Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho. Ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajji. Sambahulā āpattiyo ekāhapaṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo dvīhapaṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo tīhapaṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo catūhapaṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo pañcāhapaṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo chāhapaṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo sattāhapaṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo aṭṭhāha paṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo navāhapaṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo, so saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ yā āpattiyo dasāha paṭicchannāyo, tāsaṃ agghena samodhāna parivāsaṃ yācati. Saṅgho (tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ yā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo tāsaṃ agghena samodhāna parivāsaṃ deti. Yassāyasmato khamati, (tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ yā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo, tāsaṃ agghena samodhāna parivāsassa dānaṃ. So tuṇhassa. Yassa nakkhamati, so bhāsey‍ya.

Dutiyampi etamat‍thaṃ vadāmi. Suṇātu me -pe- so bhāsey‍ya.

Tatiyampi etamat‍thaṃ vadāmi. Suṇātu me -pe- so bhāsey‍ya.

Dinno saṅghena (tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ yā āpattiyo dasāha paṭicchannāyo tāsaṃ agghena samodhāna parivāso, khamati saṅghassa tasmā tuṇhī. Evametaṃ dhārayāmi”

Having received the probation, undertake it immediately and announce it to the Community.

Formula for Undertaking.

Parivāsaṃ samādiyāmi. Vattaṃ samādiyāmi.

Recite three times.

Formula for Announcing the Probation.

Ahaṃ bhante! sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajjiṃ. Sambahulā āpattiyo ekāhapaṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo dvīhapaṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo tīhapaṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo catūhapaṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo pañcāha paṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo chāhapaṭicchannāyo. Sambahulā āpattiyo sattāhapaṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo aṭṭhāhapaṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo navāhapaṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo, so’haṃ bhante! saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ yā āpattiyo dasāha paṭicchannāyo tāsaṃ agghena samodhāna parivāsaṃ yāciṃ. Tassa me saṅgho tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ yā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo tāsaṃ agghena samodhānaparivāsaṃ adāsi. So’haṃ parivasāmi. Vedayāmahaṃ bhante! vedayatīti maṃ saṅgho dhāretu.

Announce this three times. Thereafter, suspend (Nikkhepa) the probation, go to a suitable place, and fulfill it by undertaking it daily until the days are complete. The formula for suspension is: “Parivāsaṃ nikkhipāmi. Vattaṃ nikkhipāmi” (I suspend the probation. I suspend the duty). Recite this three times. The suspension of the duties of probation or penance may be performed in the presence of a single bhikkhu. If you forget to suspend the duty or penance before the Community within the boundary, you must go to a single bhikkhu and suspend it. If that bhikkhu is one to whom you have not previously announced your probation, you must announce it first and then suspend it. If he is a bhikkhu who was present when you announced it within the boundary, you may suspend it without announcement.

When announcing the duty or penance to a single bhikkhu, say “Vedayatīti maṃ āyasmā dhāretu”; when announcing to two bhikkhus, say “Vedayāmahaṃ āyasmantā dhārentu”; and when announcing to three bhikkhus, say “Vedayatīti maṃ āyasmanto dhārentu”.

When requesting the duties or penance, when reciting the formal act statement, and when making the announcement, use “Ekādasāhapaṭicchannāyo dvādasāhapaṭicchannāyo terasāha paṭicchannāyo cuddasāhapaṭicchannāyo” for offenses concealed from eleven to fourteen days. For offenses concealed for fifteen days, use “Pakkhapaṭicchannāyo”. For concealment from sixteen to twenty-nine days, use “Atirekapakkhapaṭicchannāyo”. For offenses concealed for one month, use “Māsapaṭicchannāyo”. Beyond that, use “Atirekamāsapaṭicchannāyo dvemāsapaṭicchannāyo atireka dvemāsapaṭicchannāyo” and so forth. For offenses concealed for one year, use “Ekasaṃvacchara paṭicchannāyo”. Beyond that, use “Atireka ekasaṃvacchara dvisaṃvacchara atireka dvisaṃvacchara” and so forth.

Upon completion of the probation, the penance (Mānatta) must be undertaken for the offenses for which probation was served as well as for those that were unconcealed. If the probation had been suspended, you must re-undertake it within the boundary and announce it to the Community before receiving the penance. Only one who is currently maintaining the undertaking of probation is eligible to receive the penance. If the penance is received while the probation remains suspended, it is not validly received. Therefore, the penance fulfilled in such a manner is fruitless. Furthermore, the days of probation may sometimes become invalid due to a 'break in the nights' (Ratticcheda). If even one day is missing from the required duration of probation, that person is not eligible to receive the penance. If the penance is taken and fulfilled without completing the probation, it is fruitless. Therefore, probation must be served with great care. If there is doubt regarding any day due to a break in the nights, the duty must be served again for those days, and the penance should only be taken once certain of completion.

Formula for Requesting Combined Penance.

Ahaṃ bhante! sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajjiṃ. Sambahulā āpattiyo ekahapaṭicchannāyo -pe- sambahulā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo. So’haṃ bhante! saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ yā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo tāsaṃ agghena samodhāna parivāsaṃ yāciṃ. Tassa me saṅgho tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ yā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo tāsaṃ agghena samodhānaparivāsaṃ adāsi. So’haṃ bhante! parivut‍tha parivāso. Ahaṃ bhante! sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajjiṃ appaṭicchannāyo. So’haṃ bhante! saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattaṃ yācāmi.

Request three times.

The Formal Act Statement for Granting Penance.

“Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho. Ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajji. Sambahulā āpattiyo ekāhapaṭicchannāyo -pe- sambahulā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo. So saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ yā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo tāsaṃ agghena samodhānaparivāsaṃ yāci. Saṅgho (tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ yā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo tāsaṃ agghena samodhānaparivāsaṃ adāsi. So parivut‍tha parivāso. Ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajjiṃ appaṭicchannāyo. So saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattaṃ yācati. Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ. Saṅgho (tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattaṃ dadey‍ya. Esā ñatti.

Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho, ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajji. Sambahulā āpattiyo ekāhapaṭicchannāyo. -pe- sambahulā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo. So saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ yā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo. Tāsaṃ agghena samodhānaparivāsaṃ yāci. Saṅgho (tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ yā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo. Tāsaṃ agghena samodhānaparivāsaṃ adāsi. So parivut‍tha parivāso. Ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajji, appaṭicchannāyo. So saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattaṃ yācati. Saṅgho (tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattaṃ deti. Yassāyasmato khamati (tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattassa dānaṃ. So tuṇhassa, yassa nakkhamati, so bhāsey‍ya.

Dutiyampi etamat‍thaṃ vadāmi. Suṇātu me -pe- so bhāsey‍ya.

Tatiyampi etamat‍thaṃ vadāmi. Suṇātu me -pe- so bhāsey‍ya.

Dinnaṃ saṅghena (tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattaṃ. Khamati saṅghassa, tasmā tuṇhī. Evametaṃ dhārayāmi.”

Upon receiving the penance, the previously undertaken probation is terminated. Now, you must undertake the penance received within the boundary and announce it to the Community.

Formula for Undertaking.

Mānattaṃ samādiyāmi. Vattaṃ samādiyāmi.

Recite three times.

Formula for Announcing the Penance.

Ahaṃ bhante sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajjiṃ. Sambahulā āpattiyo ekāhapaṭicchannāyo. Sambahulā āpattiyo dvīhapaṭicchannāyo. Sambahulā āpattiyo tīhapaṭicchannāyo. Sambahulā āpattiyo catūhapaṭicchannāyo, sambahulā āpattiyo pañcāhapaṭicchannāyo. Sambahulā āpattiyo chāhapaṭicchannāyo. Sambahulā āpattiyo sattāha paṭicchannāyo. Sambahulā āpattiyo aṭṭhāha paṭicchannāyo. Sambahulā āpattiyo navāha paṭicchannāyo. Sambahulā āpattiyo dasāha paṭicchannāyo, so’haṃ bhante! saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ yā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo tāsaṃ agghena samodhāna parivāsaṃ yāciṃ. Tassa me saṅgho tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ yā āpattiyo dasāha paṭicchannāyo tāsaṃ agghena samodhānaparivāsaṃ adāsi. So’haṃ bhante! parivuttha parivāso. Ahaṃ bhante! sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajjiṃ. Appaṭicchannāyo. So’haṃ bhante! saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattaṃ yāciṃ. Tassa me saṅgho tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattaṃ adāsi. So’haṃ mānattaṃ carāmi. Vedayāma’haṃ bhante! vedayatīti maṃ saṅgho dhāretu.

Announce this three times. Thereafter, you may suspend it if you wish, by reciting “Mānattaṃ nikkhipāmi. Vattaṃ nikkhipāmi” three times. While probation may be served where only a single bhikkhu resides, at least four bhikkhus must be present for the fulfillment of the penance. Therefore, you must go to a place where the Community is present and fulfill the penance for six days. It must be announced to the Community every day. Once the six-day penance is completed, a group of not less than twenty bhikkhus must be assembled within the boundary to perform the Act of Rehabilitation (Abbhāna). If the penance was in a state of suspension at that time, you must re-undertake and announce it before the Community before requesting rehabilitation.

Formula for Requesting Rehabilitation.

Ahaṃ bhante! sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajjiṃ. Sambahulā āpattiyo ekāhapaṭicchannāyo. -pe- sambahulā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo. So’haṃ bhante! saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ yā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo. Tāsaṃ agghena samodhānaparivāsaṃ yāciṃ. Tassa me saṅgho tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ. Yā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo tāsaṃ agghena samodhānaparivāsaṃ adāsi. So’haṃ bhante parivut‍tha parivāso. Ahaṃ bhante sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajjiṃ. Appaṭicchannāyo. So’haṃ bhante! saṃghaṃ tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattaṃ yāciṃ. Tassa me saṅgho tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattinaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattaṃ adāsi. So’haṃ bhante! ciṇṇamānatto saṅghaṃ abbhānaṃ yācāmi.

Request three times.

The Formal Act Statement for Rehabilitation.

“Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho. Ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajji. Sambahulā āpattiyo ekāhapaṭicchannāyo. -pe- sambahulā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo. So saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ yā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo tāsaṃ agghena samodhānaparivāsaṃ yāci. Saṅgho (tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ yā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo tāsaṃ agghena samodhānaparivāsaṃ adāsi. So parivut‍tha parivāso. Ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajji, appaṭicchannāyo, so saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattaṃ yāci. Saṅgho (tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattaṃ adāsi. So ciṇṇamānatto saṅghaṃ abbhānaṃ yācati. Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, saṅgho (tissaṃ) bhikkhuṃ abbhey‍ya. Esā ñatti.

Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho. Ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajji. Sambahulā āpattiyo ekāhapaṭicchannāyo, -pe- sambahulā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo, so saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ yā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo, tāsaṃ agghena samodhāna parivāsaṃ yāci. Saṅgho (tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ yā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāyo tāsaṃ agghena samodhānaparivāsaṃ adāsi. So parivut‍tha parivāso, ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajji, appaṭicchannāyo. So saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattaṃ yāci. Saṅgho (tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattaṃ adāsi. So ciṇṇamānatto saṅghaṃ abbhānaṃ yācati. Saṅgho (tissaṃ) bhikkhuṃ abbheti. Yassāyasmato khamati (tissassa) bhikkhuno abbhānaṃ. So tuṇhassa. Yassa nakkhamati, so bhāsey‍ya.

Dutiyampi etamat‍thaṃ vadāmi. Suṇātu me -pe- so bhāsey‍ya.

Tatiyampi etamat‍thaṃ vadāmi. Suṇātu me -pe- so bhāsey‍ya.

Abbhito saṅghena (tisso) bhikkhu. Khamati saṅghassa tasmā tuṇhī. Evametaṃ dhārayāmi.

Absolute Probation (suddhantaparivāsaya)

A bhikkhu who has committed many offenses but knows neither the number of offenses nor the number of days they were concealed must fulfill Absolute Probation. Considering the duration he lived without announcing his offenses, he must serve probation for that period, then undertake the six-day penance (Mānatta), and finally undergo the Act of Rehabilitation (Abbhāna).

If he has never made an announcement of offenses since his higher ordination, and offenses have occurred, he must fulfill the duty for the entire duration since his higher ordination.

Formula for Requesting Absolute Probation.

Ahaṃ bhante! sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajjiṃ. Āpattipariyantaṃ na jānāmi. Rattipariyantaṃ na jānāmi. Āpattipariyantaṃ nassarāmi. Ratti pariyantaṃ nassarāmi. Āpattipariyante vematiko. Rattipariyante vematiko. So’haṃ bhante! saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ suddhantaparivāsaṃ yācāmi.

Recite three times.

The Formal Act Statement for Granting Probation.

Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho, ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajji. Āpattipariyantaṃ na jānāti, ratti pariyantaṃ na jānāti. Āpatti pariyantaṃ nassarati. Rattipariyantaṃ nassarati, āpattipariyante vematiko, rattipariyante vematiko. So saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ suddhantaparivāsaṃ yācati. Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, saṅgho (tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ suddhantaparivāsaṃ dadey‍ya. Esā ñatti.

Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajji. Āpattipariyantaṃ na jānāti. Rattipariyantaṃ na jānāti. Āpattipariyantaṃ nassarati. Rattipariyantaṃ nassarati. Āpattipariyante vematiko. Rattipariyante vematiko. So saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ suddhantaparivāsaṃ yācati. Saṅgho (tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ suddhanta parivāsaṃ deti. Yassāyasmato khamati (tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ suddhantaparivāsassa dānaṃ. So tuṇhassa. Yassa nakkhamati. So bhāsey‍ya.

Dutiyampi etamat‍thaṃ vadāmi. Suṇātu me -pe- so bhāsey‍ya.

Tatiyampi etamat‍thaṃ vadāmi. Suṇātu me -pe- so bhāsey‍ya.

Dinno saṅghena (tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ suddhantaparivāso. Khamati saṅghassa tasmā tuṇhī. Evametaṃ dhārayāmi.

Formula for Undertaking Probation.

Parivāsaṃ samādiyāmi. Vattaṃ samādiyāmi.

Undertake by reciting three times.

Formula for Announcement.

Ahaṃ bhante! sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajjiṃ. Āpattipariyantaṃ na jānāmi. Rattipariyantaṃ na jānāmi. Āpattipariyantaṃ nassarāmi. Rattipariyantaṃ nassarāmi. Āpatti pariyante vematiko. Rattipariyante vematiko. So’haṃ bhante! saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ suddhantaparivāsaṃ yāciṃ. Tassa me saṅgho tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ suddhantaparivāsaṃ adāsi. So’haṃ parivasāmi vedayāmahaṃ bhante vedayatīti maṃ saṅgho dhāretu.

Announce this three times. When announcing to one, two, or three bhikkhus, use the appropriate grammatical variations. When suspending (Nikkhepa), say “Parivāsaṃ nikkhipāmi. Vattaṃ nikkhipāmi” three times. The suspension is effective even with one of these phrases. The Commentary states: "Even with one word, the probation is suspended here; but with two, it is well-suspended" (Ekapadenā’pi vet‍tha nikkhitto hoti parivāso. Dvīhi pana sunikkhitto yeva). Having taken probation in this manner, go to a secluded place, reflect on your past, and fulfill the duty for a sufficient number of days to remove all doubt before taking the penance (Mānatta). Since one with many offenses may also have unconcealed offenses, when taking the penance, combine the concealed offenses for which probation was served with any unconcealed offenses, and take the penance for all of them. If the probation was in a state of suspension, you must first re-undertake and announce the probation within the boundary before requesting the penance from the Community.

Formula for Requesting Penance.

Ahaṃ bhante! sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajjiṃ. Āpattipariyantaṃ na jānāmi. Rattipariyantaṃ na jānāmi. Āpattipariyantaṃ nassarāmi. Rattipariyantaṃ nassarāmi. Āpattipariyante vematiko. Rattipariyante vematiko. So’haṃ bhante! saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ suddhantaparivāsaṃ yāciṃ. Tassa me saṅgho tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ suddhantaparivāsaṃ adāsi. So’haṃ bhante! parivuttha parivāso. Ahaṃ bhante! sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajjiṃ. Appaṭicchannāyo. So’haṃ bhante! saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattaṃ yācāmi.

Recite three times.

The Formal Act Statement for Granting Penance.

Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho, ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajji. Āpattipariyantaṃ na jānāti. Rattipariyantaṃ na jānāti. Āpattipariyantaṃ nassarati. Rattipariyantaṃ nassarati. Āpattipariyante vematiko. Rattipariyante vematiko. So saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ suddhanta parivāsaṃ yāci. Saṅgho (tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ suddhantaparivāsaṃ adāsi. So parivut‍thaparivāso. Ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajjiṃ appaṭicchannāyo. So saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattaṃ yācati. Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, saṅgho (tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattaṃ dadey‍ya. Esā ñatti.

Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho. Ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajji. Āpattipariyantaṃ na jānāti. Rattipariyantaṃ na jānāti. Āpattipariyantaṃ nassarati. Rattipariyantaṃ nassarati. Āpattipariyante vematiko. Rattipariyante vematiko. So saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ suddhantaparivāsaṃ yāci. Saṅgho (tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ suddhantaparivāsaṃ adāsi. So parivut‍thaparivāso. Ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajjiṃ appaṭicchannāyo. So saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattaṃ yācati. Saṅgho (tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattaṃ deti. Yassāyasmato khamati, (tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattassa dānaṃ. So tuṇhassa. Yassa nakkhamati so bhāsey‍ya.

Dutiyampi etamat‍thaṃ vadāmi. Suṇātu me -pe- so bhāsey‍ya.

Tatiyampi etamat‍thaṃ vadāmi. Suṇātu me -pe- so bhāsey‍ya.

Dinnaṃ saṅghena (tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattaṃ. Khamati saṅghassa tasmā tuṇhī. Evametaṃ dhārayāmi.

After receiving the penance, while still within the boundary, arrange the upper robe over one shoulder, sit in the squatting posture, pay homage to the Community, and undertake the penance by reciting “Mānattaṃ samādiyāmi. Vattaṃ samādiyāmi” three times, then announce it to the Community.

Formula for Announcing the Penance.

Ahaṃ bhante! sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajjiṃ. Āpattipariyantaṃ na jānāmi. Rattipariyantaṃ na jānāmi. Āpattipariyantaṃ nassarāmi. Rattipariyantaṃ nassarāmi. Āpattipariyante vematiko. Ratti pariyante vematiko. So’haṃ bhante! saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ suddhantaparivāsaṃ yāciṃ. Tassa me saṅgho tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ suddhantaparivāsaṃ adāsi. So’haṃ bhante! parivuttha parivāso. Ahaṃ bhante sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajjiṃ appaṭicchannāyo. So’haṃ bhante! saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattaṃ yāciṃ. Tassa me saṅgho tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattaṃ adāsi. So’haṃ mānattaṃ carāmi. Vedayāma’haṃ bhante vedayatīti maṃ saṅgho dhāretu.

Announce this three times. Thereafter, paying homage to the Community, say “Mānattaṃ nikkhipāmi. Vattaṃ nikkhipāmi” three times to suspend the penance. Then, go and reside in a monastery where there are not less than four bhikkhus, fulfilling the penance for six days before undergoing the Act of Rehabilitation (Abbhāna). To undergo rehabilitation, assemble a group of not less than twenty bhikkhus within the boundary. If the penance was in a state of suspension, re-undertake it within the boundary, announce it to the Community, and then request rehabilitation.

Formula for Requesting Rehabilitation for Absolute Probation.

Ahaṃ bhante! sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajjiṃ. Āpattipariyantaṃ na jānāmi. Rattipariyantaṃ na jānāmi. Āpattipariyantaṃ nassarāmi. Rattipariyantaṃ nassarāmi. Āpattipariyante vematiko. Rattipariyante vematiko. So’haṃ bhante! saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ suddhantaparivāsaṃ yāciṃ. Tassa me saṅgho tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ suddhantaparivāsaṃ adāsi. So’haṃ bhante parivutthaparivāso. Ahaṃ bhante! sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajjiṃ appaṭicchannāyo. So’haṃ bhante! saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattaṃ yāciṃ. Tassa me saṅgho tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattaṃ adāsi. So’haṃ bhante! ciṇṇamānatto saṅghaṃ abbhānaṃ yācāmi.

Arrange the upper robe over one shoulder, sit in the squatting posture, pay homage to the Community, and request rehabilitation by reciting this formula three times.

The Formal Act Statement for Rehabilitation for Absolute Probation.

Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho. Ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajji. Āpattipariyantaṃ na jānāti. Rattipariyantaṃ na jānāti. Āpattipariyantaṃ nassarati. Rattipariyantaṃ nassarati. Āpattipariyante vematiko. Rattipariyante vematiko. So saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ suddhantaparivāsaṃ yāci. Saṅgho (tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ suddhantaparivāsaṃ adāsi. So parivut‍thaparivāso. Ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajjiṃ appaṭicchannāyo. So saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattaṃ yāci. Saṅgho (tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattaṃ adāsi. So ciṇṇamānatto saṅghaṃ abbhānaṃ yācati. Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ. Saṅgho tissaṃ bhikkhuṃ abbhey‍ya. Esā ñatti.

Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho, ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajji. Āpattipariyantaṃ na jānāti. Rattipariyantaṃ na jānāti. Āpattipariyantaṃ nassarati. Rattipariyantaṃ nassarati. Āpattipariyante vematiko. Rattipariyante vematiko. So saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ suddhantaparivāsaṃ yāci. Saṅgho (tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ suddhantaparivāsaṃ adāsi. So parivut‍thaparivāso, ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu sambahulā saṅghādisesā āpattiyo āpajjiṃ appaṭicchannāyo. So saṅghaṃ tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattaṃ yāci. Saṅgho (tissassa) bhikkhuno tāsaṃ sambahulānaṃ āpattīnaṃ paṭicchannānañca appaṭicchannānañca chārattaṃ mānattaṃ adāsi. So ciṇṇamānatto saṅghaṃ abbhānaṃ yācati. Saṅgho (tissaṃ) bhikkhuṃ abbheti. Yassāyasmato khamati (tissassa) bhikkhuno abbhānaṃ. So tuṇhassa. Yassa nakkhamati. So bhāsey‍ya.

Dutiyampi etamat‍thaṃ vadāmi. Suṇātu me -pe- so bhāsey‍ya.

Tatiyampi etamat‍thaṃ vadāmi. Suṇātu me -pe- so bhāsey‍ya.

Abbhito saṅghena (tisso) bhikkhu. Khamati saṅghassa tasmā tuṇhī. Evametaṃ dhārayāmi.

There are numerous methods in the Vinaya for obtaining purification from grave offenses (Garukāpatti). Three methods are shown here, which are currently in use among bhikkhus. Through these three methods, purification from all grave offenses can be achieved. The Formal Act Statements provided here use the name ‘Tissa’. In performing a Vinaya Act for a bhikkhu, his actual name should be substituted wherever ‘Tissa’ appears in the Formal Act Statement. If there is a difficulty in doing so, the act-proclaimer may temporarily designate the name of the candidate as ‘Tissa’ and proceed with the statement as written. To designate the name, the act-proclaimer should state:

"Venerable Sirs, for the ease of proclaiming the Formal Act Statement, I designate the name ‘Tissa’ for the bhikkhu [Name], setting aside his former name. May the Community be aware of this. Let the candidate also be aware."

In this manner, the name must be designated. Once the name is designated, the Formal Act Statement must be proclaimed exactly as it appears in the book.

Matters to be Known Regarding the Fulfillment of Vatta and Mānatta.

According to the Western system, a day consists of twenty-four hours. According to the astrological system, a day consists of sixty ghaṭikā. In the Western system, the beginning and end of a day occur at midnight. In the astrological system, the beginning of the day is considered from sunrise. According to the Vinaya method, the beginning of the day is considered from dawn (aruṇodaya).

“Sūrassodayato pubbuṭṭhitaraṃsi siyāruṇo”

In the Abhidhānappadīpikā, it is stated that the first rising ray of the sun before it appears is called 'aruṇa'. 'Aruṇa' is also a name for a light reddish color. That name is given to the first rising ray of the sun because that ray possesses a light reddish hue. That light is visible in the eastern sky about half an hour before sunrise. With the appearance of that light in the sky, according to the Vinaya method, the passing of the previous day and the beginning of the present day are considered.

When fulfilling vatta and mānatta, a day is counted not by dwelling under the undertaking for the full number of hours in a day, but by being under the undertaking at the time of dawn. Regardless of which hour of the twenty-four hours of the day the vatta is undertaken, if it is maintained until dawn, it is considered as one day gained. If a bhikkhu who has undertaken the vatta in the morning relinquishes (nikkhepa) the vatta even a moment before dawn, that bhikkhu does not gain the day. Here, the counting of days is done at dawn.

During the time of dwelling having undertaken vatta and mānatta, if even a bhikkhu traveling at a distance is seen, or if the voice of a bhikkhu is heard, an announcement (ārocana) must be made to that bhikkhu. If it is not done, an interruption of the night (ratticcheda) occurs. There is also an offense of wrong-doing (dukkaṭa) due to the breach of duty (vattabheda). The meaning of 'ratticcheda' is that the day does not become a day that is counted. If one makes an effort to announce but is unable to do so, there is no offense, but ratticcheda still occurs. Therefore, it is difficult to fulfill the vatta in places where many bhikkhus come and go, or in places where bhikkhus traveling at a distance are visible. Because there are bhikkhus traveling at a distance and because they are visible, it is also difficult to fulfill the vatta during the daytime.

The vatta cannot be fulfilled in a place where there are no bhikkhus at all. For parivāsa to be fulfilled, at least one other bhikkhu must be present. For mānatta to be fulfilled, a group of no fewer than four bhikkhus must be present. Therefore, if there are no other bhikkhus in the monastery where you reside, you must go to a monastery where there are bhikkhus, or you must invite bhikkhus to the monastery where you reside. It is also difficult to fulfill the vatta in monasteries situated near main roads where vehicles constantly travel, because when bhikkhus traveling in vehicles are seen, one does not get the opportunity to announce to them. If the monastery where the bhikkhu fulfilling the vatta resides is a suitable place for it, he should fulfill the vatta there. If not, he should go to a suitable monastery and fulfill the vatta. If you see bhikkhus traveling at a distance and cannot make the announcement, ratticcheda will occur; therefore, undertake the vatta during the night and relinquish (nikkhepa) it after dawn. Do so until the required number of days is completed.

The Method of Conduct for Bhikkhus Fulfilling Penance.

There are numerous duties to be observed by bhikkhus fulfilling probation (parivāsa) and penance (mānatta). The breach of some of these results in the interruption of nights (ratticcheda) and an offense of wrong-doing (dukkaṭa). The breach of others results only in an offense of wrong-doing. Among these, the factors causing the interruption of nights must be most carefully considered. Even if the duties are fulfilled, if an interruption of nights occurs—even unknowingly—the days are not completed, and if rehabilitation (abbhāna) is performed, the bhikkhu does not attain purification. Therefore, one must take special care to avoid the interruption of nights.

“Tayo kho upāli! pārivāsikassa bhikkhuno ratticchedā sahavāso, vippavāso, anārocanā ime kho upāli! tayo pārivāsikassa bhikkhuno ratticchedā”

Thus, in the Cūḷavaggapāḷi, three factors are declared that cause the interruption of nights for a bhikkhu on probation: co-residence (sahavāsa), living apart (vippavāsa), and non-announcement (anārocanā). 'Co-residence' means sleeping under one roof in the same building with a bhikkhu of natural standing or with another bhikkhu fulfilling probation or penance. It should be noted that 'sleeping' does not strictly mean falling asleep. Whether one has fallen asleep or not, remaining in a sleeping posture is what is meant here by 'sleeping'. If one is sitting and leaning against a pillar or a wall, even if one falls asleep, it is not considered 'sleeping'. When two bhikkhus reside in one building, if one bhikkhu is sleeping while the other remains in a different posture, there is no interruption of nights. If both sleep at the same time, an interruption of nights occurs. If both are fulfilling probation, the interruption of nights occurs for both. If another bhikkhu enters and sleeps in the building where a bhikkhu fulfilling his duties is sleeping, without the latter's knowledge, an interruption of nights occurs for the one on probation; however, because he was unaware, there is no offense.

'Living apart' means passing the dawn in a place where there are no bhikkhus, separated from other bhikkhus. 'Non-announcement' means failing to announce one's status upon seeing a bhikkhu or hearing a bhikkhu's voice. If a bhikkhu on probation is on this side of a river and sees a bhikkhu on the other side, and is unable to announce his status to him, an interruption of nights occurs.

“Cattāro kho upāli! mānattacārikassa bhikkhuno ratticchedā, sahavāso, vippavāso, anārocanā, ūne gaṇe caraṇaṃ. Ime kho upāli! cattāro mānattacārikassa bhikkhuno ratticchedā”

Thus, four factors are declared as causes for the interruption of nights for a bhikkhu undergoing penance: co-residence, living apart, non-announcement, and practicing in an incomplete group. Practicing in an incomplete group means practicing the penance in a group of fewer than four bhikkhus.

During the period of fulfilling probation or penance, the bhikkhu must not act as a preceptor (upajjhāya) for higher ordination. He must not recite the formal act (kammavācā). If a situation arises where these must be done, he must first set aside (nikkhepa) the duty. He must not give dependence (nissaya). If there are pupils (antevāsika) who have taken dependence from him, he must tell those bhikkhus: "I am performing a disciplinary act. You must not perform duties for me. You must not ask me for permission to enter the village, etc." He must not be a preceptor for novices (sāmaṇera). If there are novices who have previously taken him as preceptor, he must tell them the same. If they continue to perform duties out of respect for their teacher even after being told, there is no offense in accepting. He must not fall into the same offense for which he undertook the duty, nor into any similar offense.

He must not act as the head of a monastery. On an Observance day (uposatha), he must not recite the Pātimokkha nor accept an invitation to recite it. He must not make accusations against other bhikkhus. Even if he is senior in ordination, he must not walk in front of bhikkhus of natural standing. He must not sit on a high seat. He must not accept invitations from donors and lead other bhikkhus. When traveling with the Community, he must walk alone, maintaining a distance of twelve cubits. When sitting in the dining hall or similar places, he must take the last seat. He must not sit on a seat while bhikkhus of natural standing are sitting on the ground. He must not sit on a high seat while bhikkhus of natural standing are on low seats. For residence in the monastery, he must take the most inferior lodging. When the Community assembles for the Observance (uposatha) or the Invitation (pavāraṇā), he must announce his duty. During that period, he must not fall again into an offense requiring probation or penance. He must not fall into any other such offense. If he falls into another grave offense (garukāpatti) while fulfilling the duty, he must fulfill the probation and penance again from the beginning. Details regarding this should be learned from the Cūḷavagga Pāḷi and the Vinaya Commentary.

If one fulfills the probation without setting it aside, there is no fault in not re-announcing to the bhikkhus to whom it has already been announced. If a visiting bhikkhu is encountered, the announcement must be made. If one sets aside the duty daily and undertakes it only during the night, the announcement must be made every day upon each undertaking. As for the penance (mānatta), even if it is not set aside, it must be announced to the Community every day.

A Matter to be Observed when Granting Probation and Penance.

In certain instances, owing to a shortage of bhikkhus, it may be necessary to include bhikkhus who have previously undertaken probation or penance in the proceedings for granting the same to others. In such circumstances, one must first relinquish the probation or penance; one should not participate in granting probation or penance to another bhikkhu while still observing it. If a bhikkhu who is observing probation or penance is counted toward the quorum of the Community, the probation or penance being granted is not validly effected. It is an invalid act. This applies similarly to the performance of the act of rehabilitation.

pārivāsika catuttho ce bhikkhave! parivāsaṃ dadeyya. Mūlāya paṭikasseyya. Mānattaṃ dadeyya. Vīso abbheyya. Akammaṃ na ca karaṇīyaṃ. Mūlāya paṭikassanāraha catuttho ce bhikkhave! parivāsaṃ dadeyya. Mūlāya paṭikasseyya, mānattaṃ dadeyya, viso abbheyya, akammaṃ na ca karaṇīyaṃ. Mānattāraha catuttho ce bhikkhave! parivāsaṃ dadeyya, mūlāya paṭikasseyya, mānattaṃ dadeyya, vīso abbheyya, akammaṃ na ca karaṇīyaṃ. Mānattacārikacatuttho ce bhikkhave! parivāsaṃ dadeyya, mūlāya paṭikasseyya, mānattaṃ dadeyya. Vīso abbheyya, akammaṃ na ca karaṇīyaṃ, abbhānāraha catuttho ce bhikkhave! parivāsaṃ dadeyya, mūlāya paṭikasseyya, mānattaṃ dadeyya, vīso abbheyya. Akammaṃ na ca karaṇīyaṃ.

This is a teaching found in the Campeyya Khandhaka of the Mahāvagga Pāḷi.

Probation for Sectarians

If a naked sectarian comes seeking the Going-forth and Higher Ordination in the Buddha’s Dispensation, give him the Going-forth and let him observe a four-month probation. He should be granted Higher Ordination only after he has completed and fulfilled the four-month probation. The one who was formerly a member of another sect, having approached the Community, having arranged his upper robe over one shoulder, having paid homage to the feet of the bhikkhus, and having sat in the squatting position, should request probation thus:

Ahaṃ bhante! (tisso) aññatitthiyapubbo imasmiṃ dhammavinaye ākaṅkhāmi upasampadaṃ. So’haṃ bhante! saṅghaṃ cattāro māse parivāsaṃ yācāmi. Dutiyampi ahaṃ bhante -pe- tatiyampi ahaṃ bhante -pe- parivāsaṃ yācāmi.

The Formal Act for Granting Probation.

Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho. Ayaṃ (tisso) aññatit‍thiyapubbo imasmiṃ dhammavinaye ākaṅkhati upasampadaṃ. So saṅghaṃ cattāro māse parivāsaṃ yācati. Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ. Saṅgho (tissassa) aññatit‍thiyapubbassa cattāro māse parivāsaṃ dadey‍ya, esā ñatti.

Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho, ayaṃ (tisso) aññatit‍thiya pubbo imasmiṃ dhammavinaye ākaṅkhati upasampadaṃ, so saṅghaṃ cattāro māse parivāsaṃ yācati. Saṅgho (tissassa) aññatit‍thiya pubbassa cattāro māse parivāsaṃ deti. Yassāyasmato khamati (tissassa) aññatit‍thiya pubbassa cattāro māse parivāsassa dānaṃ, so tuṇhassa. Yassa nakkhamati so bhāsey‍ya. Dinno saṅghena (tissassa) aññatit‍thiya pubbassa cattāro māse parivāso. Khamati saṅghassa tasmā tuṇhī. Evametaṃ dhārayāmi.

After he has satisfied the bhikkhus during the four-month probation, the one who was formerly a member of another sect should be given Higher Ordination. Further details should be known from the Mahakhandhaka.

The Act of Assigning a Site for a Hut

The bhikkhu building the hut should clear the site for the hut, approach the Community, arrange his upper robe over one shoulder, pay homage at the feet of the senior bhikkhus, sit on his heels, raise his joined palms, and request the Community three times as follows:

“Ahaṃ bhante! saññācikāya kuṭiṃ kattukāmo assāmikaṃ attuddesaṃ. So’haṃ bhante! saṅghaṃ kuṭivatthuolokanaṃ yācāmi.”

When the bhikkhu building the hut has thus requested, if the Community wishes to go and inspect the site, it is well. If not, the Community should authorize competent bhikkhus who are capable of discerning whether there are obstructions or a lack of open space, and send them to inspect the site.

The Formal Act of Authorization.

Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho, ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu saññācikāya kuṭiṃ kattukāmo assāmikaṃ attuddesaṃ. So saṅghaṃ kuṭivat‍thu olokanaṃ yācati. Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, saṅgho (buddharakkhitañca dhammarakkhitañca) bhikkhu sammanney‍ya (tissassa) bhikkhuno kuṭivat‍thuṃ oloketaṃ: esā ñatti.

Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho, ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu saññācikāya kuṭiṃ kattukāmo assāmikaṃ attuddesaṃ. So saṅghaṃ kuṭivat‍thu olokanaṃ yācati. Saṅgho (buddharakkhitañca dhammarakkhitañca) bhikkhu sammannati (tissassa) bhikkhuno kuṭivat‍thuṃ oloketuṃ. Yassāyasmato khamati (buddharakkhitassa ca dhammarakkhitassa ca) bhikkhūnaṃ sammuti (tissassa) bhikkhuno kuṭivat‍thuṃ oloketuṃ, so tuṇhassa. Yassa nakkhamati, so bhāsey‍ya.

Sammatā saṅghena (buddharakkhito ca dhammarakkhito ca) bhikkhu (tissassa) bhikkhuno kuṭivat‍thu oloketuṃ, khamati saṅghassa, tasmā tuṇhī, evametaṃ dhārayāmi.

The authorized bhikkhus should go to the site where the hut is to be built and investigate whether it is a place with obstructions, and whether there is enough open space for a cart yoked with two oxen to pass around the hut. If it is a place with obstructions or lacks the required open space, they should inform the bhikkhu building the hut that he should not build there. If the site is suitable and appropriate for building a hut, they should inform the Community. Thereafter, the bhikkhu building the hut should approach the Community, pay homage at the feet of the senior bhikkhus, sit on his heels, raise his joined palms, and request the assignment of the site for the hut.

The Request for the Assignment of the Site for a Hut.

“Ahaṃ bhante! saññacikāya kuṭiṃ kattukāmo assāmikaṃ attuddesaṃ. So’haṃ bhante! saṅghaṃ kuṭivatthudesanaṃ yācāmi. Dutiyampi -pe- tatiyampi -pe- yācāmi.”

The Formal Act for Assigning the Site for a Hut.

Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho, ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu saññācikāya kuṭiṃ kattukāmo assāmikaṃ attuddesaṃ, so saṅghaṃ kuṭivat‍thudesanaṃ yācati. Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, saṅgho (tissassa) bhikkhuno kuṭivat‍thu desey‍ya. Esā ñatti.

Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho, ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu saññācikāya kuṭiṃ kattukāmo assāmikaṃ attuddesaṃ. So saṅghaṃ kuṭivat‍thu desanaṃ yācati. Saṅgho (tissassa) bhikkhuno kuṭivat‍tuṃ deseti. Yassāyasmato khamati (tissassa) bhikkhuno kuṭivat‍thu desanā, so tuṇhassa, yassa nakkhamati so bhāsey‍ya.

Desitaṃ saṅghena (tissassa) bhikkhuno kuṭivat‍thu. Khamati saṅghassa. Tasmā tuṇhī. Evametaṃ dhārayāmi.

The purpose of having the site assigned is to obtain the formal approval of the Community to build a hut or a dwelling. A bhikkhu who builds a hut for his own use with materials he himself has begged for, without having the site assigned, commits an offense of Sanghadisesa.

The Assignment of a Site for a Large Dwelling.

A bhikkhu who is building or having a large dwelling built with a benefactor must also have the site assigned by the Community before proceeding. If he builds the dwelling without doing so, he commits an offense of Sanghadisesa.

The Request for Inspection of the Site.

Ahaṃ bhante! mahallakaṃ vihāraṃ kattukāmo sassāmikaṃ attuddesaṃ. So’haṃ bhante! saṅghaṃ vihāravatthu olokanaṃ yācāmi. Dutiyampi -pe- tatiyampi -pe-

The Formal Act of Authorization for Inspecting the Site for a Dwelling.

Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho, ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu mahallakaṃ vihāraṃ kattukāmo sassāmikaṃ attuddesaṃ. So saṅghaṃ vihāravat‍thu olokanaṃ yācati. Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ. Saṅgho (buddharakkhitañca dhammarakkhitañca) bhikkhu sammanney‍ya (tissassa) bhikkhuno vihāravat‍thuṃ oloketuṃ. Esā ñatti.

Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho, ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu mahallakaṃ vihāraṃ kattukāmo sassāmikaṃ attuddesaṃ. So saṅghaṃ vihāravat‍thu vilokanaṃ yācati. Saṅgho (buddharakkhitañca dhammarakkhitañca) bhikkhu sammannati (tissassa) bhikkhuno vihāravat‍tuṃ oloketuṃ yassāyasmato khamati (buddharakkhitassa ca dhammarakkhitassa ca) bhikkhūnaṃ sammuti (tissassa) bhikkhuno vihāravat‍thuṃ oloketuṃ so tuṇhassa. Yassa nakkhamati. So bhāsey‍ya.

Sammatā saṅghena (buddharakkhito ca dhammarakkhito ca) bhikkhu (tissassa) bhikkhuno vihāravat‍thuṃ oloketuṃ. Khamati saṅghassa, tasmā tuṇhī. Evametaṃ dhārayāmi.

The Request for the Assignment of the Site for a Dwelling.

“Ahaṃ bhante! mahallakaṃ vihāraṃ kattukāmo sassāmikaṃ attuddesaṃ, so’haṃ bhante! saṅghaṃ vihāravatthu desanaṃ yācāmi. Dutiyampi -pe- tatiyampi -pe-.”

The Formal Act for Assigning the Site for a Dwelling.

Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho. Ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu mahallakaṃ vihāraṃ kattukāmo sassāmikaṃ attuddesaṃ. So saṅghaṃ vihāravat‍thu desanaṃ yācati. Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, saṅgho (tissassa) bhikkhuno vihāravat‍thuṃ desey‍ya. Esā ñatti.

Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho, ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu mahallakaṃ vihāraṃ kattukāmo sassāmikaṃ attuddesaṃ. So saṅghaṃ vihāravat‍thu desanaṃ yācati. Saṅgho (tissassa) bhikkhuno vihāravat‍thuṃ deseti. Yassāyasmato khamati (tissassa) bhikkhuno vihāravat‍thu desanā, so tuṇhassa. Yassa nakkhamati so bhāsey‍ya.

Desitaṃ saṅghena (tissassa) bhikkhuno vihāravat‍thu. Khamati saṅghassa tasmā tuṇhī. Evametaṃ dhārayāmi.

The Formal Act of Admonition

If a certain bhikkhu attempts to cause a schism in the Community, or persists in taking up a matter that leads to a schism, the bhikkhus who meet that bhikkhu attempting the schism should advise him: "Venerable, do not attempt to cause a schism in the harmonious Community." You should advise him thus a second and a third time. Bhikkhus who hear reports of such a bhikkhu should also seek out the bhikkhu attempting the schism and advise him. If he hears the advice but does not relinquish his position, he commits an offence of wrongdoing (dukkaṭa). Bhikkhus who hear of the bhikkhu attempting the schism but do not advise him also commit an offence of wrongdoing (dukkaṭa). If the bhikkhu attempting the schism does not relinquish his position even after being advised, the other bhikkhus should bring him, even by force, into the midst of the Community and perform the Formal Act of Admonition (samanubhāsana kamma).

The Transaction Statement.

Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho. Ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu samaggassa saṅghassa bhedāya parakkamati. So taṃ vat‍thuṃ na paṭinissajjati. Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, saṅgho (tissaṃ) bhikkhuṃ samanubhāsey‍ya tassa vat‍thussa paṭinissaggāya. Esā ñatti.

Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho, ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu samaggassa saṅghassa bhedāya parakkamati. So taṃ vat‍thuṃ na paṭinissajjati. Saṅgho (tissaṃ) bhikkhuṃ samanubhāsati tassa vat‍thussa paṭinissaggāya. Yassāyasmato khamati (tissassa) bhikkhuno samanubhāsanā tassa vat‍thussa paṭinissaggāya, so tuṇhassa. Yassa nakkhamati, so bhāsey‍ya.

Dutiyampi etamat‍thaṃ vadāmi. Suṇātu me -pe- so bhāsey‍ya.

Tatiyampi etamat‍thaṃ vadāmi. Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho, ayaṃ (tisso) bhikkhu samaggassa saṅghassa bhedāya parakkamati. So taṃ vat‍thuṃ na paṭinissajjati. Saṅgho (tissaṃ) bhikkhuṃ samanubhāsati tassa vat‍thussa paṭinissaggāya. Yassāyasmato khamati (tissassa) bhikkhuno samanubhāsanā tassa vat‍thussa paṭinissaggāya. So tuṇhassa. Yassa nakkhamati. So bhāsey‍ya

Samanubhaṭṭho saṅghena (tisso) bhikkhu tassa vat‍thussa paṭinissaggāya. Khamati saṅghassa, tasmā tuṇhī. Evametaṃ dhārayāmi.

In performing this Formal Act of Admonition, if the bhikkhu attempting the schism does not relinquish his view, an offence of wrongdoing (dukkaṭa) is incurred at the conclusion of the Motion (ñatti). At the conclusion of the first proclamation (anusāvana), a grave offence (thullaccaya) is incurred. At the conclusion of the second proclamation, a grave offence (thullaccaya) is also incurred. At the conclusion of the transaction statement, an offence entailing initial and subsequent meetings of the Community (saṅghādisesa) is incurred.

The Formal Act of Admonition for Bhikkhus who side with Schismatics.

Bhikkhus who side with a bhikkhu attempting a schism should also be advised by other bhikkhus not to do so. You should advise them a second and a third time; if they do not accept the advice, they commit an offence of wrongdoing (dukkaṭa). Bhikkhus who know of this but do not advise them also commit an offence of wrongdoing (dukkaṭa). If they do not accept the advice, you should bring those bhikkhus into the midst of the Community, even by force, and advise them. If they still do not accept, the Formal Act of Admonition should be performed.

The Transaction Statement.

Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho. (buddharakkhito ca dhammarakkhito ca) bhikkhu (tissassa) bhikkhuno saṅghabhedāya parakkamantassa anuvattakā vaggavādakā te taṃ vat‍thuṃ na paṭinissajjanti. Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, saṅgho (buddharakkhitañca dhammarakkhitañca) bhikkhu samanubhāsey‍ya tassa vat‍thussa paṭinissaggāya. Esā ñatti.

Suṇātu me bhante! saṅgho, (buddharakkhito ca dhammarakkhito ca) bhikkhu (tissassa) bhikkhuno saṅghabhedāya parakkamantassa anuvattakā vaggavādakā. Te taṃ vat‍thuṃ na paṭinissajjanti. Saṅgho (buddharakkhitañca dhammarakkhitañca) bhikkhu samanubhāsati tassa vat‍thussa paṭinissaggāya. Yassāyasmato khamati (buddharakkhitassa ca dhammarakkhitassa ca) bhikkhūnaṃ samanubhāsanā tassa vat‍thussa paṭinissaggāya. So tuṇhassa. Yassa nakkhamati, so bhāsey‍ya.

Dutiyampi etamat‍thaṃ vadāmi. Suṇātu me -pe- so bhāsey‍ya. Tatiyampi etamat‍thaṃ vadāmi. Suṇātu me -pe- so bhāsey‍ya.

Samanubhaṭṭho saṅghena (buddharakkhito ca dhammarakkhito ca) bhikkhu tassa vat‍thussa paṭinissaggāya. Khamati saṅghassa, tasmā tuṇhī. Evametaṃ dhārayāmi.

The Formal Act of Admonition for Difficult-to-admonish Bhikkhus.

When a bhikkhu is being advised by other bhikkhus according to the training rules laid down by the Buddha, such as: "Venerable, this should be done, this should not be done, you have fallen into such-and-such an offence," if that bhikkhu does not allow himself to be spoken to, saying: "Venerables, do not say anything to me, whether good or bad; you should mind your own business. I will not say anything to you, whether good or bad; do not say anything to me," then the bhikkhus should advise that bhikkhu not to act in that manner. You should advise him thus a second and a third time. If he does not accept the advice, he commits an offence of wrongdoing (dukkaṭa).

If the bhikkhus know of this but do not advise him, they also commit an offence of wrongdoing (dukkaṭa).
